115 年 9 月海關進出口貿易初步統計 
一、進出口貿易變動 
（一）出口 
9 月出口 872.2 億美元，創歷年單月新高，較上月增 5.9%，較上年
同月增 60.9%(按新臺幣計算增 68.2%)；第3 季出口 2,449.2 億美元，
較上年同季增 44.6%；累計 1 至 9 月 6,615.5 億美元，較上年同期增
46.2%(按新臺幣計算增 49.1%)。 
（二）進口 
9 月進口 635.9 億美元，亦創歷年單月新高，較上月增 5.8%，較上
年同月增 51.7%(按新臺幣計算增 58.6%)；第 3 季進口 1,818.0 億美
元，較上年同季增 44.3%；累計 1 至 9 月 5,010.0 億美元，較上年同
期增 41.3%(按新臺幣計算增 43.8%)。 
（三）出入超 
9 月出超 236.3 億美元，為單月新高，較上年同月增 113.4 億美元；
累計 1 至 9 月出超 1,605.5 億美元，較上年同期增 625.4 億美元。

二、出口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
由於創新科技應用商機持續擴展，推升相關供應鏈出貨動能，加上
消費性電子新品備貨需求與產品價格上漲效應之挹注， 9 月出口較
上年同月增 330.0 億美元(+60.9%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 資通與視聽產品出口 391.3 億美元(歷年單月次高)， 較上年同月增
198.6 億美元(+1.0 倍)， 其中電腦及其附屬單元增157.4 億美元(+1.1
倍)、儲存媒體增 17.6 億美元(+2.2 倍)、交換器及路由器增 10.5
億美元(+1.1 倍)。 
2. 電子零組件出口 311.3 億美元(歷年單月次高)， 較上年同月增97.1
億美元(+45.3%)，其中積體電路增 93.9 億美元(+46.1%)、印刷電
路增 1.4 億美元(+33.7%)、 電容器及電阻器增0.8 億美元(+32.0%)。 
3. 礦產品出口 22.6 億美元，較上年同月增 10.5 億美元(+86.4%)，其
中石油煉製品增 10.1 億美元(+99.0%)。 
4. 機械出口 27.4 億美元(歷年單月次高)，較上年同月增 6.4 億美元
(+30.7%)，其中生產半導體等機械增 2.7 億美元(+61.0%)、未列名
具特殊功能機械增 0.6 億美元(+73.6%)。 
5. 基本金屬及其製品出口 26.9 億美元，較上年同月增 5.0 億美元
(+22.9%)，其中銅及其製品增 2.5 億美元(+65.1%)、鋼鐵及其製品
增 1.4 億美元(+11.7%)。

1. 第 3 季出口較上年同季增 755.9 億美元(+44.6%)，以資通與視聽產
品增 367.6 億美元(+55.0%)、電子零組件增 308.4 億美元(+51.2%)
增加較多。 
2. 累計 1 至 9 月出口較上年同期增 2,089.8 億美元(+46.2%)，其中資
通與視聽產品增 1,222.2 億美元(+72.4%)、電子零組件增 726.6 億
美元(+45.7%)。

等出現結構性轉變，爰另予併計。

三、進口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
隨 AI 產業鏈全球分工與出口引申需求擴增，電子零組件、資通產品
及半導體設備進口維持強勁，加上國際農工原料行情處於高檔，9
月進口較上年同月增 216.7 億美元(+51.7%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 農工原料進口440.5 億美元， 較上年同月增163.9億美元(+59.3%)，
其中電子零組件增 112.6 億美元(+82.5%)、電腦之零附件增 19.4
億美元(+1.5 倍)、礦產品增 17.1 億美元(+38.9%)。 
2. 資本設備進口 128.0 億美元， 較上年同月增 36.0 億美元(+39.1%)，
其中資通設備增 18.4 億美元(+75.0%)、半導體設備增 12.4 億美元
(+34.9%)，交通運輸設備減 1.4 億美元(-46.4%)。 
3. 消費品進口 62.1 億美元，較上年同月增 16.5 億美元(+36.0%)，其
中固態非揮發性儲存裝置增 16.4 億美元(+5.3 倍)、筆電增 0.5 億
美元(+43.8%)，藥粧用品減 1.2 億美元(-16.6%)。

四、主要出口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
9 月對東協出口創新高，對美國、陸港為單月次高 
1. 對美國出口 274.6 億美元，較上年同月增 141.5 億美元(+1.1 倍)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 124.1 億美元(+1.3 倍)、 電子零組件增10.1
億美元(+1.3 倍)、機械增 2.2 億美元(+42.3%)。 
2. 對中國大陸與香港出口 206.2 億美元，較上年同月增 47.8 億美元
(+30.2%)，其中電子零組件增 34.9 億美元(+30.5%)、資通與視聽
產品增 5.7 億美元(+39.0%)、機械增 1.9 億美元(+43.7%)。 
3. 對東協出口 188.8 億美元，較上年同月增 91.2 億美元(+93.4%)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 49.7 億美元(+2.2 倍)、電子零組件增 32.0
億美元(+63.3%)、礦產品增 4.8 億美元(+1.4 倍)。 
4. 對歐洲出口 45.3 億美元，較上年同月增 7.7 億美元(+20.6%)，其
中資通與視聽產品增 5.7 億美元(+34.1%)、電子零組件增 2.2 億美
元(+56.6%)，基本金屬及其製品減 0.8 億美元(-20.5%)。 
5. 對日本出口 30.7 億美元，較上年同月增 3.6 億美元(+13.4%)，其
中電子零組件增 1.5 億美元(+11.0%)、基本金屬及其製品增 0.6 億
美元(+32.5%)、機械增 0.5 億美元(+29.1%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 9 月對美國出口 2,077.8 億美元，較上年同期增 773.1 億美
元(+59.3%)，對中國大陸與香港出口 1,600.9 億美元，增 355.3 億美
元(+28.5%)， 對東協出口1,339.6 億美元 ， 增463.6 億美元(+52.9%)，
對歐洲出口 421.8 億美元 ， 增138.4 億美元(+48.8%)， 對日本出口277.7
億美元，增 58.3 億美元(+26.6%)。

五、主要進口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
9 月自中國大陸與香港進口 135.0 億美元 ， 較上年同月增50.1 億美元
(+59.1%)，自南韓進口 116.3 億美元，增52.6 億美元(+82.5%)，自東
協進口 106.0 億美元，增 52.6 億美元(+98.4%)，自日本進口 64.3 億
美元，增 17.7 億美元(+38.0%)，自歐洲進口 50.2 億美元，增 3.5 億
美元(+7.4%)，自美國進口 48.1 億美元，增 6.5 億美元(+15.5%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 9 月自主要國家/地區進口與上年同期比較 ， 自東協增77.6%、
自南韓增 65.8%、自中國大陸與香港增 42.8%、自日本增 25.7%、自
美國增 21.4%、自歐洲增 10.8%。

六、主要國家/地區貿易概況 
（一）主要國家/地區今年 1-8 月外貿景氣普遍走升，以南韓(1-9 月)出口年
增 56.8%最為強勁，香港、新加坡亦分別年增 49.0%、39.8%；中國
大陸、美國出口分別年增 19.3%及 14.6%，德國(1-7 月)、日本各增
9.7%、8.3%。 
（二）主要國家/地區進口同步上揚，累計 1-8 月以香港進口年增 50.5%較
突出，新加坡、中國大陸分別年增 37.3%、27.0%，南韓(1-9 月)、德
國(1-7 月)分別增 19.7%、10.0%，日本、美國各增 7.8%、4.2%。

### Page 7

7
圖 5 2026 年主要國家/地區出進口年增率
七、綜合分析
（一）受惠於人工智慧(AI)、高效能運算及雲端服務等應用商機持續擴展，
加以步入傳統外銷旺季 、產品價格上漲效應，9 月出口 872.2 億美元，
年增 60.9%，連續 35 個月正成長；進口635.9 億美元，年增51.7%，
主因 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口引申需求擴張，及國際原物料行情居
高；出、進口值及出超 236.3 億美元齊創單月新高。累計 1 至 9 月出
口年增 46.2%、進口增 41.3%。
（二）9 月主要貨品出口普遍較上年同月成長，以資通與視聽產品增 1.0 倍
最佳，包括顯示卡、伺服器、儲存媒體、 交換器及路由器等出口均
呈倍數增加 ；其次為礦產品增 86.4%，主因全球油品供應偏緊支撐價
格維持高檔；電子零組件隨 AI 應用及消費性電子新品備貨需求升溫，
增 45.3%，機械、電機產品亦各增 3 成上下。累計 1 至 9 月資通與視
聽產品、電子零組件出口併計增 59.5%，占總出口比重 79.0%。

（三）主要市場方面，9 月對美國、東協出口值分創歷年單月次高、新高，
年增率各為 1.1 倍及 93.4%，合計對總出口增加貢獻達 7 成；對中國
大陸與香港、歐洲、日本出口各年增 30.2%、20.6%、13.4%。累計 1
至 9 月對五大市場出口值皆為歷年同期最高，其中對美國、東協均
提前刷新全年紀錄 ， 分別年增59.3%、52.9%，對歐洲出口年增48.8%，
對陸港、日本各增 28.5%、26.6%。
（四）展望未來，全球貿易政策及地緣政治情勢變化等，仍增添總體 經濟
不確定性，惟隨人工智慧應用 持續深化，國際雲端服務業者 及各國
加速 AI 基礎建設投資，帶動半導體、伺服器等相關供應鏈需求擴增，
加以科技新品備貨、中國大陸及歐美年底採購旺季將屆， 均有利挹
注我國外銷動能，第 4 季出口可望延續成長態勢。

中東緊張暫緩，風險資產全面反攻
2026 年 5 月
4 月全球主要股市報酬全面轉正，與 3 月跌勢形成鮮明對比，AI 題材再度成為主軸，重燃市場熱
度，風險資產動能強勁回溫。以美元計價 4 月表現觀察，新興亞洲與新興市場上漲分別上漲 17.4%
與 14.5%，表現最為亮麗，主要受台灣與南韓在 AI 半導體供應鏈帶動下的強勁買盤支撐；標普 500
的 4 月漲幅也創 2020 年 11 月以來最佳月表現 。 然而 ， 拉丁美洲與東協市因與科技題材連結度較低，
使其期間漲幅相對緩和。不過，MSCI 拉丁美洲指數今年前 4 個月的漲幅仍高達 17.3%，為所有主要

股市之冠。
隨著市場風險接受度提高，全球主要債市4 月也同步反彈。新興當地債券單月上漲2.79%表現最
佳，主要因美元指數當月由約 100 降至近 98，使新興當地債換算回美元後的價格水漲船高。波動較
大的亞洲高收益債、新興美元債券、全球高收益債表現也有所表現。全球投資級公司債、全球主權債
券等債券雖亦分別上漲 1.36%、1.25%，但幅度上相對落後。
4 月市場大幅反彈的核心驅動力來自三方面：其一，投資人對中東戰事可能導致通膨上揚的疑慮
明顯鈍化；其二，AI 主軸再度成為主導行情的關鍵主題，包括 Alphabet 雲端與 AI 業務需求暢旺帶
動半導體類股大漲；其三，總經基本面韌性意外優於預期，整體勞動市場仍穩定。然而，霍爾木茲海
峽運輸尚未完全恢復，中東戰事變數仍存，地緣風險仍是市場最大不確定性來源。
根據彭博 5/4 的統計，今年 Q1 與 Q2 EPS 年增率均可望突破兩成，我們預期 2026 年標普 500
企業獲利增長動能持續 ，AI 仍是核心驅動主軸。儘管短期股市可能因中東戰事帶來通膨隱憂而波動，

但我們認為企業基本面強度足以抵銷中東衝突與政策不確定性 ，而企業強勁的獲利成長將可望為全球
股市中期走勢帶來支撐。
債市方面，受到美伊戰爭影響，4 月的 FOMC 會議會後聲明偏鷹，壓抑市場降息預期，考量後續
美伊和談時程仍有不確定性 ，預期長天期公債殖利率波動風險將加劇 。 由於目前美國中短天期公債殖
利率顯著高於過去 10 年均值，且中短期債券到期期間較短，具備資金靈活運用彈性，因此建議在債
券天期選擇上 ， 以中短天期美國公債及高評級投資級債為主 ， 並以平衡投資組合波動角度做適當配置 。

美國股市：美股盈餘增長強勁，AI 主題重獲市場青睞
4 月美股雖受伊朗戰爭不確定性干擾，在財報優異及 AI 產業高速成長帶動下，標普 500 指數續
創歷史新高。指數上揚主要由輝達、Alphabet、亞馬遜、博通、蘋果、微軟、超微、英特爾、美光
等科技股帶動，表現相對弱勢的族群包括埃克森美孚、雪佛龍、嬌生、默克藥廠、亞培、ServiceNow、
AT&T、洛克希德馬丁、雷神等，市場焦點已經從能源、國防回歸 AI 主題。
美國通膨墊高，但經濟表現仍具韌性，支持聯準會更長時間維持利率。4 月 ISM 製造業 PMI 持
平於 52.7，連續第四個月維持擴張區間。其中供應商交貨指數從 58.9 升至 60.6，反映伊朗戰爭與航

運通道風險升高，導致供應鏈壓力再度升溫；客戶庫存指數降至 39.1，持續偏低；新訂單指數小幅
上升至 54.1，訂單動能尚具支撐，積壓訂單由 54.4 大幅降至 51.4，新出口訂單進一步下滑至 47.9，
顯示外需偏弱；價格指數從 78.3 攀升至 84.6；就業指數降至 46.4，顯示企業尚未恢復積極擴編意願。
故製造業雖維持擴張 ， 但由於庫存已經偏低 ，若戰事持續恐將加速推升通膨。4 月份 ISM 服務業 PMI
指數由前月的 54.0 下降至 53.6，低於市場預期的 53.7，新訂單指數由前月的 60.6 下降至 53.5，低
於市場預期的 57.3，支付價格指數持平於 70.7，物價雖仍在高檔但並未惡化。同時預期新任聯準會
主席 Warsh 上台後，要等到現任主席鮑威爾完全辭去 Fed 理事職務，才會有較大的空間與明確度去
推進「相信 AI 將帶來生產力提振、進而支持降息」的政策論述，故降息時間或許延後，未來利率將
更長時間維持不變。
AI 帶動科技股財報表現優異。預估第一季標普 500 企業盈餘年增可達到 23.9%，且第二季盈餘

增長仍維持近兩成水準，科技板塊受惠於 AI，仍是帶動美股企業獲利增長主軸，但到了第二季，能
源價格維持高檔下將推升能源產業獲利，且若伊朗戰事無法落幕，預料各種原物料價格都將受影響走
高，標普 500 企業獲利成長性並不看淡。
美國金融股第一季財報普遍優於預期，對消費惡化與私募基金流動性的擔憂已經淡化。由於股票
與商品市場交投熱絡，帶動承銷與交易收入顯著成長，各大銀行放款規模持續擴張，淨利息收入成長
優於預期。消費信用方面，信用卡支出與消費貸款餘額仍具韌性，整體資產品質良好。
目前標普 500 在指數續創新高下本益比來到 26 倍、股價淨值比 5.36 倍，短期評價偏高，且通
膨風險逐漸增加、伊朗戰爭的不確定性仍在，雖美國科技龍頭在 AI 產業具備領先優勢，但短期需留
意市場過熱及估值偏高風險，建議以長線角度分批布局。

歐洲股市：歐洲央行態度謹慎，川普加徵歐洲汽車關稅
4 月歐股受能源價格大漲衝擊，雖財報優異但科技股比重相對美股較低，泛歐 600 指數緩步墊高。
指數上揚主要由艾司摩爾、西門子、西門子能源、匯豐、艾波比、英飛凌、施耐德電機、諾基亞、瑞
銀集團、諾和諾德等企業帶動，表現相對弱勢的族群包括殼牌、德意志電信、阿斯特捷利康、諾華、
葛蘭素史克、聯合利華、依視路羅薩奧蒂卡、赫力昂、挪威國家石油、BP 等，市場焦點已經從能源、
國防題材回歸科技與金融股。
4 月歐元區 服務業受到物價飆漲與能源支出暴增的雙重打擊， 服務 業採購經理人指數 急挫至

47.4，創疫情後最差表現；然而製造業採購經理人指數則在新訂單改善(尤其法國)及避險備貨需求的
支撐下，逆勢攀升至 52.2，然而就業人數持續萎縮，反映企業在高通膨與需求不確定性下普遍採取
縮編策略，經濟放緩格局明顯。法國製造業 PMI 攀升至 52.8，主要得益於新訂單走高，反映需求復
甦；德國製造業 PMI 雖達 51.4，增長動能依賴企業拉升庫存，能否延續仍須觀察。
儘管歐洲央行在 4 月利率決策會議按兵不動，但此舉是反映需要更多時間凝聚升息共識，並蒐集
能源衝擊對通膨的實際影響數據 。歐洲央行總裁透露委員會內部曾認真討論升息的可能性，維持不動
的理由包括：近期數據仍接近3 月預測、貨幣政策在能源價格上漲前已處於適當位置、金融與信貸條
件已開始收緊，目前局勢「偏離」原先基準情境，通膨上行風險與經濟成長下行風險均在加劇，但歐
洲經濟尚未進入停滯。市場預期 ECB 將於 2026 年升息 2 碼，甚至不排除於年底或於明年升息第 3
碼，但考量歐洲通膨仍與伊朗戰事息息相關，若能源價格回落、需求走弱，則升息時點可能延後；若

能源價格繼續在高檔盤旋、通膨持續性跡象提早浮現，不排除6 月即會啟動第一次升息。英格蘭銀行
(英國央行)在最新利率決策會議中維持利率不變，但整體立場偏向謹慎，市場同樣預期未來半年可能
有 2 碼升息幅度。近期 ECB 與 BOE 的升息預期已帶動歐元、英鎊轉強。
川普於 5/1 宣布，由於歐盟不遵守貿易協議，將對歐盟進口汽車與卡車加徵 25%關稅(不包含英
國)。由於歐洲議會在 3/26 已有條件通過歐美貿易協議的相關立法，故川普調整汽車業關稅從 15%
到 25%，可能引發美歐貿易戰再次啟動，接下來預料歐盟祭出關稅反制措施且展開新一輪談判。
泛歐 600 指數目前本益比為 16.87，股價淨值比為 2.39，目前面臨能源價格仍在高檔、川普對歐
盟汽車業加徵關稅，短期風險增加。然而考量評價合理、伊朗戰爭持續降溫且預料美歐經過談判，終
將能達成貿易協議，歐股仍可以長線角度、作為分批佈局標的。

日本股市：日本利率按兵不動，AI 題材帶動指數創高
4 月上旬日股受地緣政治風險衝擊，所幸在 AI 題材帶動下科技股走高，日經指數更於 4 月一度
收盤站上 60,537 點，以收盤價站上 6 萬點歷史新高。市場主要由軟銀、愛德萬測試、Keyence、村
田製作所、三菱電機、三菱 UFJ 金融、東京威力科創、藤倉、鎧俠、三井住友金融等公司帶動，表
現相對弱勢的族群包括三菱商事、豐田汽車、任天堂、三井物產、永旺、武田藥品、國際石油開發帝
石、7&I、山之內製藥、東方樂園等，市場焦點回歸 AI、國防，以及升息可能帶動的金融股。

相較於歐洲汽車出口至美國關稅再度被川普調高，目前並未傳出美國可能調高日本進口汽車關稅
的訊息，顯示高市早苗先前與川普會晤帶來實質外交成效。日本進入黃金週，但高市首相選擇飛往越
南與澳洲「拼外交」，尤其將澳洲定位為「準同盟」 ，推動以「最上級」護衛艦為基礎的共同開發與輸
出，顯然高市對於國防自主有堅定決心。
日本央行 4 月利率決策會議以 6 比 3 同意將利率維持於 0.75%，三位委員認為應將政策利率調
高至 1.0%，市場對後續升息的預期明顯升溫。日本央行同時將 FY2026 核心 CPI 預測大幅上調 0.9
個百分點至+2.8%，FY2027 上調 0.3 個百分點至+2.3%，這些預測建立在油價將從每桶約 105 美元
回落至 70～80 美元的假設之上 ， 故若油價長期維持高位 ， 通膨前景可能在7 月展望報告中再度上修 ，
而對 GDP 增長預期也小幅下修。對比會後聲明透露出來的鷹派立場，總裁植田和男會後記者會發言
立場則略顯保守，持續淡化央行升息時點，強調需要時間觀察經濟及通膨發展，且提及達成央行經濟

預估的前景下降 ， 尚無法確定需要多久來評估下次升息時點 。 鑒於過於弱勢的日圓匯率可能更進一步
推高國內通膨，日本央行仍有高機率於第三季上調政策利率，若油價再度上行，甚至不排除在 6 月就
採取行動。
截至 4 月 24 日當周，外資買超日股 8,079 億日圓，2026 年以來外資對日股的累計買超金額來
到 7.97 兆日圓，改寫今年以來累計加碼金額紀錄。近期日財長與外匯最高官員相繼釋出干預匯市警
告、加上外資持續匯入且市場對 BOJ 升息的預期，日圓貶值趨勢已扭轉。近期日圓一度單日升值 3%，
顯示 160 為日本政府當局匯價的干預底線，預料近期日圓仍將於 155 至 158 附近震盪。
2026 年以來日股 TOPIX 指數受惠於 AI 題材、高市擴大財政刺激、強化軍事安全，但本益比仍
在 19.39 倍，股價淨值比在 1.77 倍。近期日圓守住 160 關卡，匯率穩定可降低投資者不確定性，預
料日本企業股票回購仍持續推進，可望為日股在全球金融市場震盪下帶來支撐。

台灣股市：強勁經濟與產業基本面支撐下的長多格局
4 月台灣股市在 AI 超級週期與全球資金的簇擁下 ， 正式跨越了四萬點 ， 本次台股的突破並非單純
的資金行情，而是具備了極為堅實的總體經濟、強健的產業鏈獲利基本面，以及法規鬆綁帶來的籌碼
結構重塑。
台灣今年初展現了令全球市場矚目的經濟爆發力。主計總處公布的第一季 GDP 成長率概估高達
13.69%，創下近 39 年來的單季最高紀錄。這股強大動能的源頭，清晰地反映在經濟部的三月份外銷
訂單數據上， 三月單月接單金額飆升至 911.2 億美元 ， 年增率高達65.9%， 連續14 個月維持正成長。

資訊通信與電子產品年增率分別高達 120.9%與 73.7%，這顯示台灣在全球 AI 基礎設施、高效能運
算（HPC）以及雲端資料中心建設中，扮演著不可替代的樞紐角色。亮眼的總經數據，為台股的高檔
估值提供了最強而有力的下檔支撐。
中經院 4 月製造業 PMI 大幅跳升 4.9 個百分點至 60.3%，連續 7 個月呈現擴張，更是近 55 個月
以來最快的擴張速度。除了 AI 與半導體的實質需求外，中東地緣政治的緊張局勢促使製造業啟動了
「防禦性提前拉貨」 。短期內這將維持製造業的熱絡，但也必須持續監控輸入性通膨的潛在風險。
宏觀數據之外，台積電於 4 月中旬召開的法說會，無疑是穩住外資與台股軍心的定海神針。財報
顯示，第一季 EPS 達 22.08 元，毛利率以 66.2%飛越財測高標。其中高效能運算（HPC）相關營收
佔比已正式突破六成 （達 61%） ，並上修了2026 全年美元營收成長目標至 30% 以上。這場法說會
不僅確認了台積電高階製程的護城河，也確立了全球 AI 伺服器供應鏈的長線能見度。

在基本面一片大好之際，金管會放寬了主動型基金與主動式 ETF 對單一個股 10%的持股上限，
從 10%放寬至 25%。這項法規鬆綁產生了極為強大的「流動性溢價」效應。過去受限於10%緊箍咒
的機構、 基金資金 ， 如今得以大舉回補台積電 、 聯發科等核心權值股 。這讓大盤指數在衝破四萬點後，
資金高度向權值龍頭集中，成為加權指數的進一步推收力量。
綜合亮眼的總經數據、良好的企業基本面，以及籌碼向大型股集中的法規紅利，我們對台股整體
的長線投資建議維持加碼。然而，目前加權指數與季線的正乖離率已來到 18%的歷史相對高位，意
味著市場隨時可能出現技術性拉回與獲利了結的賣壓。必須強調的是，牛市中的急跌與拉回屬於 「健
康的籌碼換手」 ，並非長線趨勢的反轉。面對當前乖離頗大、但長線看多的盤勢，建議投資人不追高，
待拉回後布局，預留部分現金，保留流動性以迎接拉回時的絕佳買點。

科技類股：半導體產業持續看好，分批佈局雲端巨頭
回顧 4 月份，在地緣政治影響逐漸淡化之後，華爾街對 AI 硬體的「錯失恐懼症（FOMO） 」全面
爆發。費城半導體指數上演了史無前例的連續 18 個交易日上漲，單月狂飆近 40%。這波資金浪潮不
僅將指數推向歷史新高。支撐這波半導體狂飆的是極其扎實的財報利多，不僅英特爾靠著晶圓代工與
CPU 獲利超預期迎來大復活，記憶體族群更步入了史無前例的「超級循環」 。由於AI 伺服器對 HBM
與高階 SSD 需求爆發，導致記憶體陷入結構性大缺貨。美光（Micron）與威騰（WDC）等大廠握

有絕對的定價權，這再次確認了半導體作為「AI 算力基礎設施」的長線多頭地位。
在剛公布的財報中，四大雲端巨頭交出了令人驚豔的成績單。微軟的 AI 相關年度經常性收入大
增，Google Cloud 也寫下逾 60%的驚人成長，Meta 則透過 AI 優化廣告投放，拉升利潤率，亞馬
遜（AWS）的營收增速也創下近期新高。這些數據證明，掌握龐大生態系的巨頭，已經能將 AI 算力
成功轉化為自身業務的現金流。此外，根據 2026 Q1 財報數據，四大雲端服務供應商並未因高基期
而放慢腳步，反而持續加大投入，全年資本支出（Capex）預計由原先估計的 6,000 億美元進一步增
加至近 7,000 億美元。在這波驚人的支出中除了晶片設計商外，代工的台積電、供應HBM 的三星與
海力士，以及伺服器組裝與散熱解決方案的廠商都因此受惠，這也是硬體續漲的主要原因。
然而儘管 CSP 業者的支出續增，但投資人顯然不再全面買單，市場進入「AI 變現檢驗期」 。由於
龐大的折舊壓力與變現的陣痛 ，四大 CSP 業者在財報後的股價表現各不相同 。Google 與亞馬遜成功

用現有核心業務的強勁現金流護航 AI 支出，成為資金的避風港；反觀微軟與 Meta，因資本支出預
期大幅暴增 ， 市場擔憂長期支出深不見底 ，變現遙遙無期 ，積極的佈局反而成為短期壓抑股價的包袱 。
然而四大 CSP 的 EPS 年增率依然維持在雙位數，但評價面卻因資本開支焦慮被壓低，下檔風險相對
受控，這提供了一個逢低布局的機會。
AI 產業的長多格局不變，但在進入 5 月後，市場可能面臨傳統的「Sell in M ay」季節性逆風，
且 2026 年適逢美國期中選舉，歷史經驗顯示第二、三季往往是波動最劇烈的時期。因此，我們建議
半導體類股仍是不可或缺的持股，但也應該開始逢低布局美股七巨頭等大型科技類股。 七巨頭的預估
本益比已經回落至合理區間，且具備穩健的現金流。加上隨著 Google 的 TPU、亞馬遜的 Trainium
等自研晶片部署比例提升，CSP 業者將逐步降低對高價 GPU 的依賴，這將顯著降低長期推論成本。
因此建議適時布局相關類股，來迎接下半年變現能力轉強後的估值修復行情。

AI 電力類股：資料中心用電需求倍增，有利電力基礎建設發展
IEA(國際能源署)發布 2026 年 Key Questions on Energy and AI 報告，指出受到 AI 快速發展影
響，全球用電需求將急遽成長，有利於相關電力基礎建設發展。IEA 指出，全球資料中心用電量在 2024
至 2025 年間成長逾 15%，年增約 70 太瓦時至 485 太瓦時，占全球電力需求略超過 1.5%，未來幾
年用電需求更將進一步加速，IEA 預估全球資料中心用電將從 2024 年的 415 太瓦時倍增至 2030 年
的 950 太瓦時，屆時將占全球電力需求的 3%，AI 專用資料中心用電量更將成長三倍至 465 太瓦時。

驅動電力需求暴增的核心因素是 AI 伺服器功耗密度的指數級攀升。以 Nvidia 為例，2020 年
Ampere 架構機架功耗密度約 13 千瓦時，當前 Blackwell 架構已達 130 千瓦時，下一代 Rubin 架構
將躍升至 600 千瓦時， 屆時單一機架尖峰用電量將等同約 65 戶家庭 。在 AI 伺服器功耗上升的同時，
大型科技巨頭資本支出也持續攀升，為 AI 伺服器帶來更多需求，2025 年科技巨頭資本支出合計已超
過 4,000 億美元，2026 年預計再增 75%至 7,150 億美元，IEA 更進一步預估 2026 至 2030 年間資
料中心累計投資將達 3.9 兆美元，隨資料中心大量建置，AI 用電需求將隨之攀升。
此外，IEA 指出儘管單一 AI 任務的能耗效率正以前所未見的速度改善，單一任務功耗每年約可
降至十分之一，然而新興高耗能應用如影片生成、推理及代理式 AI 也持續推陳出新，這些新功能的
能耗可達到簡單文字生成的數百至數千倍 ，導致即便 AI 效率持續提升，整體能耗需求仍然有增無減。

在 AI 用電需求大增的同時，目前電力基礎建設供給端各環節均出現嚴重瓶頸，相關關鍵零組件
因產能不足，導致交期持續延長，包含變壓器交期平均需二至三年，燃氣輪機交期長達五年，電網併
聯在美國多數地區也須排隊長達五至十年。以德州 ERCOT 獨立電網為例，資料中心併網排隊量從
2024 年 12 月的 63 吉瓦時暴增至 2026 年 1 月逾 230 吉瓦時，但該州歷史尖峰負載僅 85 吉瓦時，
顯示電力需求遠超既有電網承載量。可預見隨電力需求持續攀升，相關電力基礎建設上中下游未來訂
單積壓量都可望保持高水位，並隨公司產能陸續開出，逐步轉換為公司營收貢獻。
展望後市，IEA 預估全球資料中心發電量至 2030 年將倍增至超過 1,000 太瓦時，其中再生能源
與天然氣各占約三分之一，分別達 360 及 340 太瓦時，核能預料將從 75 太瓦時成長至 120 太瓦時，
故各類相關零組件包含燃氣輪機、變壓器、核能及儲能等子產業都可望受惠這一 AI 用電趨勢，隨 AI
電力需求持續上升，對於各類電力上中下游供應鏈都可正向看待。

能源類股：海峽受阻導致油價居高不下；阿聯酋退出 OPEC 有利戰後供給恢復
西德州原油期貨價格 4 月再度攀升至每桶 100 美元之上，主因經荷莫茲海峽的石油出口持續受
阻，加上美伊談判持續未見明顯進展，同時期間內美國總統川普已數次拒絕伊朗所提出之協議，美國
對伊朗的封鎖行動也持續進行，故在全球石油供需缺口維持的狀況下，全球石油庫存水位不斷下降，
為石油價格帶來上行壓力。
國際能源署 4 月月報指出，受荷莫茲海峽供應中斷影響，3 月全球石油供應暴跌 1,010 萬桶/日
至每日 9,700 萬桶，其中 OPEC+產量月減 940 萬桶/日至每日 4,240 萬桶，非 OPEC+供應月減 77

萬桶/日至每日 5,470 萬桶，儘管沙烏地西岸、阿聯酋富查伊拉港及伊拉克經土耳其傑伊漢的 ITP 管
線替代路線出口量，已從戰前每日不到 400 萬桶提升至每日 720 萬桶，然而仍無法彌補荷莫茲海峽
的供給中斷，使得中東海灣國家石油出口損失合計仍高達每日 1,300 萬桶，自開戰以來，3 月石油供
給損失已超過 3.6 億桶，國際能源署更進一步預估 4 月石油供給損失將擴大至 4.4 億桶。
在供給大幅下降的同時，國際能源署也同步下修全球石油需求預估，預計 2026 年全年需求將年
減 8 萬桶/日，較上月報告下修 73 萬桶/日，其中 3 月全球需求預計將年減 80 萬桶/日，4 月進一步
擴大至年減 230 萬桶/日，合計第二季需求預估年減 150 萬桶/日，為新冠疫情以來最大單季跌幅，
下調需求預估主因荷莫茲海峽供給中斷，導致眾多仰賴中東石油進口的亞洲塑化廠面臨斷供，在缺乏
原料的狀況下被迫降低需求。
然而儘管目前荷莫茲海峽持續受阻，導致油價居高不下，然而戰後有利油價快速下行的因子也持

續在萌芽。阿聯酋 4/28 宣布退出 OPEC 及 OPEC+，為該組織近年來最重要的成員流失。阿聯酋目
前石油產能為 485 萬桶/日 ， 然而其受限於OPEC 配額 ， 實際產量僅約360 萬桶/日 ， 兩者差距達125
萬桶/日，過去幾年來阿聯酋持續因產量受限問題，而與其他成員國出現爭執，隨阿聯酋退出 OPEC，
將有利於其提升產量上限。雖然目前因荷莫茲海峽仍處封鎖狀態，阿聯酋石油出口同樣受阻，然而一
旦戰後海峽重開，阿聯酋將可不再受 OPEC 配額約束，立即釋放約 125 桶/日的增量供給，阿聯酋國
有石油公司 ADNOC 更規劃將在 2027 年將石油產量上升至 500 萬桶/日、2029 年再進一步上升至
600 萬桶/日。此外阿聯酋退出 OPEC 亦可能引發其他成員跟進 ，使得戰後油價下行風險將顯著增加。
展望後市，當前油價雖受到海峽封鎖影響，中短線上有望持續維持在 100 美元左右震盪，然而
美伊戰事未見進一步擴大，後續油價也難以再創新高，此外若未來美伊談判出現進展，海峽運量出現

緩解，阿聯酋增產也有利戰後原油供需恢復，故我們目前對於能源類股後市中性看待。

### Page 9

黃金類股：全球央行續買黃金，惟短線金價仍受美伊戰爭不利影響
黃金價格 4 月持續在區間內震盪，黃金類股表現也相對弱勢，主因受到美伊戰爭影響，油價上漲
帶動通膨預期攀升，使得市場對於聯準會降息預期下滑，帶動配置黃金的機會成本上升，同時避險
情緒導致美元走強，也不利於黃金等以美元計價的商品資產表現。此外，受到戰爭影響，土耳其動
用黃金儲備以支應外匯與流動性需求，短線拋售黃金也導致金價承壓。
世界黃金協會數據顯示，2026 年 3 月各國央行申報合計淨賣出 30 公噸黃金，主要就是受到土
耳其拋售 60 公噸所拖累，受此影響，合計第一季全球央行申報黃金淨購買量僅有 2 頓。然而若看加

計未申報買盤量，則是完全不同的狀況，根據世界黃金協會調查顯示，2026 年第一季全球央行黃金
購買量加計未通報買盤後，合計淨購買 244 噸，為自 2024 年第四季以來的新高，同時顯著高於過
去五年平均購買水準的 228 噸，顯示檯面下的世界央行購金趨勢仍未改變。
另一方面，在高盛 4 月 29 日發布的最新報告中，維持了對於 2026 年底黃金目標價 5,400 美
元/盎司的預測。高盛指出，目前金價主要驅動因素包括全球央行持續推進外匯儲備多元化、目前相
對偏低的投機性部位回歸常態化，以及聯準會預計在年內降息 50 個基點等三大支撐。在央行需求方
面，高盛指出，在其央行會議調查中，約 70%的受訪央行預期全球黃金儲備將繼續上升。在風險評
估方面，高盛認為短線風險偏向下行，主要因荷莫茲海峽封鎖若持續 ，可能觸發債市或股市修正，
進而引發黃金部位遭到清算， 然而中長期風險則偏向上行，因 伊朗事件與更廣泛的地緣政治發展 ，
如格陵蘭及委內瑞拉議題，可能加速資金轉向黃金配置。
另一方面，來自穩定幣發行商 Tether 的黃金買盤也值得關注。截至 2026 年 3 月底，Tether 為

支撐 USDT 儲備而持有的黃金市值上升至 198 億美元，約為 132 公噸，較 2025 年 12 月底的 126
公噸再增加約 6 公噸 ，此外 Tether 另持有 22 公噸黃金以支撐其黃金代幣 XAUT， 兩項產品合計持倉
達約 154 公噸 ， 此規模已足以排進全球前20 名黃金持有者 。JPMorgan 貴金屬研究主管 Greg Shearer
指出，Tether 的購金行為已對金價產生實質性影響，其 2025 年全年購買量僅次於波蘭央行，並超越
所有其他央行，顯示加密貨幣機構儲備已成為黃金新一個重要買盤來源
展望後市，中長線而言央行增持黃金的結構性趨勢未變，美伊衝突加上格陵蘭、委內瑞拉等地緣
政治風險，也都有望加速各國對黃金的分散配置需求 ，使得黃金有望獲得更多來自央行買盤的支撐。
然而短線上，在美伊戰爭尚未底定前，黃金機會成本上升及部分央行拋售壓力仍將壓抑短線走勢，
故預估黃金將在區間內震盪，目前對於黃金類股後市抱持中性看法。

固定收益：華許入主 Fed 機率高，美債波動料加大
FOMC 在 4/29 的會議再度將聯邦基金利率目標區間維持在 3.50-3.75%。然而，理事 Miran 主
張降息一碼，而會後聲明中「傾於寬鬆」(easing bias)的措辭也遭三位地區主席(Cleveland 的
Hammack、Minneapolis 的 Kashkari 與 Dallas 的 Logan )反對 ，會議表決出現 4 票異議 ，創 FOMC
逾 30 年來委員立場最分歧的局面。前述三位地區聯儲主席反對寬鬆的措辭，導致市場對 Fed 後續降
息預期顯著弱化。

此外，川普提名的下任 Fed 主席 Kevin Warsh(華許)於 4/29 參議院銀行委員會審查通過，預計
於 5/11 當週進行全院表決。目前參議院共和黨、民主黨的議員比例為 53：47(含兩名傾向民主黨的
無黨籍議員)，由於除了 Tillis 以外的共和黨參議員均未公開就華許的資格表達反對，未來在參院全院
的表決中，華許也可望取得過半票數通過該任命案。若時程安排得宜，料可於 5/15 鮑爾主席任滿後
接掌 Fed。值得留意的是，鮑爾在 4 月的記者會表示將繼續留任理事會，這將導致未來的 FOMC 將
出現新舊主席並存的罕見現象。
綜觀華許的主要貨幣政策主張為「降息」 +「縮表」 ，兩者在貨幣政策方向呈現相反的效果，且
對不同天期的債券影響亦有差異： 「降息」對於雖使債券殖利率下修，但對短端影響較大；而「縮表」
量化緊縮(QT)雖屬貨幣緊縮政策，但堪薩斯聯儲研究(Smith & Valcarcel 2021)顯示，「縮表」推升期
限溢酬的幅度大於短期利率。兩者同時施行可能造成導致未來美債殖利率曲線淨陡峭化。話雖如此，

美國貨幣政策需要 FOMC 票委以多數決通過，前主席 Yellen 認為華許即便就任 Fed 主席，在表決中
仍難以取得足以支持其貨幣政策路線的票數。因此，即便 Warsh 上任，其政策主張(尤其在縮表的議
題上)短期將無法付緒實現，故預期在可見的未來對美國的貨幣政策走向改變有限。
然而，Fed 主席的權力在於議程設定與對外溝通，華許在聽證會上所表達的部分想法-包括降低
FOMC 會議與記者會舉辦的頻率等，將使 FOMC 決策透明度降低，或導致美國債市波動加大。其表
達改變目前聯準會青睞的通膨指標-核心 PCE 的意圖，也可能使金融市場的不確定性增加。
預期短線債市在通膨疑慮與政策不確定性的交錯下 ， 將持續面臨殖利率高檔震盪的壓力， 但考量
美伊全面開戰可能性極低，油價的通膨疑慮料隨時間鈍化，而 Warsh 立場傾向支持降息，因此就中
期來看，美債殖利率應有走降空間。同時，我們認為中短天期債相較於長天期債券殖利率相去不遠，
投資人無需承擔較高的存續期間更可享有不錯的債息收益 ， 且中短天期到期期間較短亦有資金靈活運

用的優勢，因此建議可以中短天期美國公債/投資級債為主。

3Q26 GM Dragged by Inventory
Write-off
Despite the secular tailwinds of increasing power consumption
in AI clusters, we think near-term sentiment toward Delta could
remain lackluster post weaker 3Q26 results.
3Q26 earnings fell short on lower GM:
• Off revenue of NT$197,059mn (+8% QoQ/+31% YoY), GM contracted  by
1.6ppt QoQ to 34.1% (vs. MSe/consensus at 35.7%/36.2%), which was
dragged 2ppt lower by the inventory write-off

• Opex was well controlled at NT$34,535mn (opex ratio 17.5%), lower than our
estimate of NT$36,983mn (opex ratio 18.4%) and consensus of NT$37,129mn
(opex ratio 18.0%)
• Operating profit came in at NT$32,620mn (+7% QoQ/+31% YoY; OPM
16.6%), 7% lower  than MSe of NT$34,992mn (OPM 17.4%) and 13% lower
than consensus of NT$37,677mn (OPM 18.2%).
• With non-op gains of NT$1,046mn (including FX gain of NT$415mn), net
profit was NT$23,495mn (-7% QoQ/+26% YoY; EPS NT$9.04), 8%/15% short

of MSe/consensus at NT$25,554mn (EPS NT$9.84)/NT$27,611mn (EPS NT
$10.40).
Our view:
• We believe near-term sentiment toward the stock will stay soft, though the
weak financial performance in 3Q26 was mainly impacted by component
supply tightness
• We believe Delta should grow its revenue by 10% QoQ with margin
expansion in 4Q26, and will continue to benefit from the increasing power
consumption of AI clusters in 2027

Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Document 124: 261029_ms_vanguard-DG

### Page 1

M
Idea
VSMC could cause  high-single digit (%) dilution to 2027 GM – above many
investors’ 3-5% expectation: On Vanguard’s latest earnings call, management noted
a successful trial run of VisionPower Semiconductor Manufacturing Company, its JV
with NXP Semiconductors. It confirmed that interposers are on track for mass
production  in 1Q27. We see revenue upside from the ramp – but depreciation could
heavily pressure margins. Management previously guided that VSMC could break

even at 30kwpm, more likely in late 2028, in our view (vs. current 8-inch margin at
30-35%). VSMC  capacity is scheduled at 44kwpm, ~100kwpm 8-inch equivalent,
about one-third of current 8-inch capacity (280kwpm). On the other hand, we think
new capacity may only run at <5kwpm in 1Q27 and <20kwpm by end-2027;
depreciation reflects 44kwpm (VSMC's full capacity) for infrastructure, clean room,
and construction. We therefore project that VSMC's gross margin dilution in full-

year 2027 could be more severe than many expect.
Despite strong customer commitment, VSMC could still dilute the corporate
margin average for at least the next five years: Since management previously
guided that VSMC could break even at 30kwpm, we view its break-even point as late
2028 or 1H29. It will then take years for GM to rise from 0% to 30-40%, even if the
capacity is fully booked. We thus expect a drag on corporate average GM for at least

the next five years. The good news: VSMC demand is skewed to high-margin
businesses –  interposers, industrial, and automotive – so the margin improvement
could be smoother  vs. peers like Hua Hong.
2027 might bring double-digit wafer price hikes and AI-related revenue at 20-
30% of the mix: During its latest earnings call, UMC guided for a 10% blended
wafer ASP increase in 2027. Given larger exposure to AI server PMIC, industrial, and
automotive, we expect Vanguard to raise 8-inch wafer prices 10-15% for the full year.

Given some customer pushback to front-loaded hikes, the price hike might be split –
twice a year, each 5-10%. Also, the company would still have L TAs expiring in 2027,
so we don’t expect aggressive wafer ASP guidance for 1Q27. With new interposer
production, we believe AI-related revenue could reach 20-30% of total revenue.
Down to UW from EW: The margin impact is larger than we initially expected and
could last 5-10 years.  Our new price target implies 2.8x our 2027e BVPS.

2
Vanguard could still show strong revenue/margin
performance for 2H26
4Q26 should maintain strong growth: We expect Vanguard to sustain strong
performance into 4Q26, supported by resilient demand from the industrial and
automotive end markets. In addition to a favorable demand outlook, ongoing capacity
optimization initiatives, particularly the transition from thicker-wire to thinner-wire
manufacturing processes, should enhance production efficiency, improve capacity

utilization, and support higher output. These operational improvements, coupled with a
favorable product mix and steady order momentum, could further underpin revenue
growth and profitability in 4Q26. We now expect Vanguard 8-inch process margin to reach
34-36% in both 4Q26 and 1Q27.

Key questions to ask during the upcoming earnings call on November 6:
1.  Price hike into 2027: How much of the incremental cost could Vanguard pass
through  to customers? Could we see double-digit apples-to-apples price hikes into
2027 despite the expiration of some L TAs?

2.  AI-related revenue: How much could  AI related revenue grow in 2027 if we
include both AI server PMIC and interposer production?
3.  Singapore fab – margin dilution and capacity allocation: How could  margin be
affected by VSMC in both 2027 and 2028? How much would depreciation be in
2027 and 2028? How would the company allocate 12-inch capacity between
interposers, NXP, and other customers?
4.  Consumer demand: Has the company seen any slowdown in consumer demand?

And how soon could that capacity be filled up by other sources of demand?
5.  Further capacity expansion: Might the company have further plans to expand 8-
inch capacity or second Singapore fab since the utilization rate has been reaching
>90%?

6
Valuation  methodology
We lower our price target (base case value) to NT$135 from NT$180:  We factor in our
earnings estimate changes for 2027-28 and also lower the intermediate growth rate
assumption from 13% to 10.5% to reflect the long-term margin dilution.
We continue to derive our price target from a residual income model, which we believe
best captures the stock's long-term value. Our key assumptions other than the
intermediate growth rate are unchanged, including a cost of equity of 9.2% (beta of 1.2,

equity risk premium of 6% and risk-free rate of 2%),  and a terminal growth rate of 4.0%.
We also lower our bull and bear case values from NT$260 and NT$110 to NT$195 and NT
$80, accordingly, implying 4.0x and 1.7x our 2027e BVPS.

MS Rating
▪ We expect 2026 capex to increase beyond
2025's high level given Singapore fab

expansion, leading to long-term margin
pressure.
▪ We expect PMIC wafer price hikes in the
industry in response to strong demand from
AI. Vanguard could beneﬁt from TSMC
mature node consolidation as well as
interposer production.
▪ The new Singapore fab could cause high-
single-digit margin dilution to the corporate
average in 2027.
▪ VSMC's 12-inch capacity is all fully booked;
production will start to ramp in 2027 with
depreciation pressure during the early stage.

▪ Our UW rating is mostly because of the
margin pressure.

NT$195.00
4.0x base case 2027e BVPS
Healthy semi demand: Auto semi demand is
exceptionally strong; inventory is digested
quickly, and DDI for smartphones and large

panels shows robust growth. We forecast a
>30% revenue CAGR in 2025-28.
Competition from peers eases. We assume:
1) global semi sales growth of >20% and
foundry industry growth of >15% in 2026; 2)
better-than-expected demand for 8"
capacity; and 3) power demand for 5G
tracking ahead of schedule.
NT$135.00
2.8x base case 2027e BVPS
Semi growth slowdown: Demand has
started to pick up for LDDIC and PMIC,
while auto semis inventory digestion is
reaching an end. We forecast a 25% revenue

CAGR in 2025-28. Vanguard starts to
reverse pricing discounts to customers in
view of stronger demand and some rush
orders in the short run. We assume: 1) global
semi sales show growth in 2026; and 2) the
foundry industry shows single-digit growth
in 2026.
NT$80.00
1.7x base case 2027e BVPS
Semi growth decelerates: Auto semi demand
weakens markedly; power semi inventory
levels remain elevated. Smartphone DDI
shows a worse-than-expected decline.
Intensiﬁed competition and new Chinese

capacity dampen the overall pricing
environment. We forecast <10% revenue
CAGR in 2025-28. We assume: 1) overall
semi industry continues to decelerate in
2026 and foundry growth is stagnant; and 2)
demand for 8" capacity is weaker than
expected owing to faster product migration.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Lite-On Tech

Upgrade to Buy; 3Q26 beat, better margin; 
solid AI outlook; lift PO to NT$288

Rating Change: BUY | PO: 288.00 TWD | Price: 240.50 TWD

Upgrade to Buy and raise PO to NT$288  
We upgrade Lite-On Tech (LoT) to Buy from Neutral with a higher PO of NT$288 (18x 
2027E EPS; prior NT$285 with valuation unchanged). We turn more positive on LoT 
after two straight quarters of results beat, especially with margin surprise. We lift 2026-
28E EPS by 1-8% (Exhibit 1), with still solid AI power-supply demand/order. Our 2026-
28E EPS is 4-7% above consensus (Exhibit 2). We expect the market to react more 
positively to LoT, with 3Q26 results beat, vs. Delta with 3Q26 results miss (see Robert 
Cheng’s Delta 3Q26 note), amid similar positive comments on long-term AI market 
demand. 
3Q26 beat: better margins, despite lighter sales 
LoT’s 3Q26 GM/OPM of 25.9%/15.1% came in better than BofAe of 25.3%/13.5% and 
consensus of 25.1%/13.6%, despite lighter sales (3%/5% below BofAe/consensus). We 
view the QoQ GM/OPM decline from record high in 2Q26 as largely expected as mgmt. 
had indicated at 2Q26 meeting that 1H26 GM of 24.7% represented a more sustainable 
level. Besides AI, better margins were also helped by improving product mix in IT/CE (i.e. 
higher mix from high-end ICT and LEO satellite) and higher automation level.  
4Q26 sales up QoQ; 2H26 GM above 1H; rising AI mix 
Similar to Delta, LoT also sees short-term shipment uncertainties/push-outs due to 
component shortage and power/electricity shortage for AI datacenter infrastructure. 
Nevertheless, the CEO remains long-term positive on AI demand. He also expects easing 
component/power constraints from 4Q26. Yet, mgmt. expects 4Q26 cloud sales to grow 
QoQ/YoY, led by ASIC/GPU/AMD projects, and 2H26 GM to be above 1H26 level (BofAe: 
26.3% in 4Q26, vs. 25.9%/24.7% in 3Q26/1H26). The CEO indicated 800VDC power rack 
mass shipment in 1Q27, after sample shipment in 4Q26. Mgmt. expects AI sales mix at 
30%+ in 2026 and 40-50% in 2027. This time, LoT raised 2026 capex to NT$19.7bn 
(prior NT$18.0bn) and sees higher level in 2027 (BofAe: NT$20.0bn).

Key Metrics

Company Sector 
Electronics Manufacturing Services

Company Description 
Lite-On Tech is a leading PC and server power-supply maker 
globally. Besides PC/server power-supply, its key products 
include optoelectronics (photo-coupler, LED lighting, 
outdoor lighting, auto lighting) and power-supply for game 
console and industrial. Key applications include AIoT, 5G, 
and smart life. Its key customers include HP, Dell, Lenovo, 
Microsoft, Sony, Nintendo, and global cloud hyperscalers. 
Lite-On Tech was founded in 1975 and headquartered in 
Taiwan.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on Lite-On Tech in view of (1) its solid 
position in AI server power-supply as the second largest 
component maker globally and (2) expanding margins with 
improving product mix, rising scale, and better operating 
efficiency. We see consistently high cash dividend payout 
(over 50% since 2008) and solid balance sheet as share 
price downside supports.

### Page 3

Upgrade to Buy; raise PO to NT$288  
We upgrade Lite-On Tech (LoT) to Buy, from Neutral, with a higher PO of NT$288 (18x 
2027E EPS) from NT$285 with valuation unchanged. We turn more positive on LoT after 
two straight quarters of results beat, especially with margin surprise. We lift 2026-28E 
EPS by 1-8% (Exhibit 1) to reflect 3Q26 results beat, the latest guidance, and solid AI 
power-supply demand/order. Our 2026-28E EPS is 4-7% above consensus (Exhibit 2).  
LoT’s share price corrected 21% and 14% in the recent three months and one month, 
respectively, vs. key peer Delta’s (2308 TT; covered by Robert Cheng) -27%/-29% and 
TAIEX’s -2%/-6%. We expect more positive market reaction to LoT’s 3Q26 results beat, 
vs. Delta’s 3Q26 results miss (see Robert Cheng’s Delta 3Q26 note), amid similar 
positive comments on long-term AI market demand. LoT also has a cheaper valuation vs. 
Delta. LoT currently trades at 15x 2027E P/E, below Delta’s 24x and overall peers’ 
average of 20x (Exhibit 6).

3Q26 beat: better margins, despite lighter sales 
LoT posted 3Q26 results and held the physical analyst meeting on 30 Oct after market 
close. 3Q26 GM/OPM of 25.9%/15.1% came in better than BofAe of 25.3%/13.5% and 
consensus of 25.1%/13.6%, despite lighter sales (3%/5% below BofAe/consensus; +9% 
QoQ, +28% YoY). We view the QoQ decline in GM/OPM from the record highs in 2Q26

### Page 4

as largely expected, as management had already indicated at the 2Q26 meeting that the 
1H26 GM of 24.7% represented a more sustainable level (see our 2Q26 result note). 
Besides AI, we believe better margins were also helped by improving product mix in 
IT/CE (i.e. higher mix from high-end ICT and LEO satellite) and higher automation level. 
With non-operating gain of NT$1.1bn (including net interest income of NT$369mn and 
FX gain of NT$734mn) and higher minority interest, net income (+4% QoQ, +60% YoY) 
was 12%/11% above BofAe/consensus.

Similar comments on material/power shortage like Delta 
Similar to Delta mgmt.’s comments at 3Q26 analyst meeting, LoT also sees short-term 
shipment uncertainties/push-outs due to material/component shortage (e.g. semis, PCB, 
passives) and power/electricity shortage for AI datacenter infrastructure. Nevertheless, 
LoT’s CEO remains long-term positive on AI demand with ongoing platform/spec 
upgrade. He also expects easing component/power constraints from 4Q26, with ongoing 
strategic component procurement/preparation and customers’ robust AI demand.

4Q26 sales up QoQ; 2H26 GM above 1H; rising AI mix 
Into 4Q26, mgmt. expects cloud sales to grow both QoQ and YoY. Key drivers include (1) 
volume production of 8.5kW PSUs for ASIC applications and BBUs, next-generation 
Nvidia Vera Rubin 110kW solutions, 42-inch power shelves for AMD Helios, (2) prototype 
shipments of 800VDC power rack, and (3) continued momentum in high-end power 
applications, with shipments of power solutions for LEO satellites. the CEO also expects 
2H26 GM to be above 1H26 level. We thus project 4Q26 overall sales to grow 5% QoQ 
(+36% YoY), with GM at 26.3% (vs. 25.9%/24.7% in 3Q26/1H26). For HVDC, the CEO 
further stated 800VDC power rack mass shipment in 1Q27, after final validation and 
sample shipment in 4Q26. Mgmt. kept AI sales mix target at 30%+ in 2026 and 
potentially 40-50% in 2027. LoT raised 2026 capex to NT$19.7bn (vs. NT$18.0bn guided 
at 2Q26 analyst meeting) and expects a higher level in 2027 (BofAe: NT$20.0bn).

Initially expects 2027 sales/GM/OPM YoY increase 
Into 2027, the CEO initially expects cloud sales to grow another 70% YoY, driven by 
rising ASP and power-supply product/spec upgrade. He targets another 10% YoY sales 
growth for optoelectronics business, driven by industrial and new energy business. He 
expects LEO satellite power to grow YoY but sees flattish sales from conventional ICT 
power business. Nevertheless, he expects GM/OPM YoY expansion in 2027. We thus 
expect 2027 sales to grow 38% YoY, with GM/OPM increasing by 1.6ppt/1.5ppt YoY.

### Page 7

Price objective basis & risk 
Delta Electronics Inc. (DLTEF) 
Our PO of NT$2,250 is based on 30x 4Q27-3Q28E EPS, which is close to 2.0 standard 
deviations above Delta's average trading P/E since 2012. We believe the P/E multiple is 
justified, given (1) continuous strength and leading position in server power-supply and 
power choke business, (2) content/value growth with broader product offering in AI 
(artificial intelligence) business, (3) rising cooling sales by targeting top CSPs' 
customized projects, and (4) ongoing margin expansion.

The upside risks to our PO are (1) stronger-than-expected server/datacenter demand, (2) 
faster-than-expected IA recovery, and (3) faster-than-expected EV model launches by 
global brands.

The downside risks to our PO are (1) unexpected changes in policy that could impact IA 
growth in China, (2) slower-than-expected server/datacenter demand recovery, (3) 
earnings impact from unprofitable long-term investments (4) Adverse effects of 
announced USITC 337 investigation into multiple companies in the AI supply chain, 
which includes Delta Electric. 
Lite-On Tech (LOTZF) 
Our PO of NT$288 is based on 18x 2027E EPS, which is around 1.75SD above its 
historical average since 2013. We believe the P/E multiple is justified, given its solid 
market position to capture multi-year AI server power-supply market demand with 
improving product mix. Yet, we also assign a valuation discount to its key peer, given its 
late entry and a slower progress in cooling business, smaller scale, and lighter margin 
profile.

Downside risks: (1) weaker-than-expected demand from server/cloud power-supply 
business, (2) lighter margin profile for automotive LED lighting with limited ASP 
expansion, (3) pricing competition and component price increase from power-supply 
business, and (4) less order win from cooling business.

Upside risks: (1) stronger-than-expected margin profile for automotive LED lighting with 
ASP expansion, (2) faster revenue growth from server/cloud power-supply with a more 
positive datacenter market outlook, and (3) larger/stronger order win from cooling 
business.

研 究  報 告                
聯亞(3081.TT) 
產業：電子–通信網路 
矽光需求持續強勁，更多設備投產與新增後段外包夥伴帶動產能倍增  
 投資評價與建議 
受惠CW Laser出貨量持續增加、新購顯影設備逐步進駐以及後段代工廠產能
開出，預估稅後EPS為21.01/49.3/97.15元，考量NPO和CPO技術逐步成熟且
聯亞具備多波⻑雷射領先技術，從2028年獲利評價，給予40倍本益比。 
 重點評論分析 
矽光產品出貨增加 ， 3Q26營收成⻑ ， 主要客⼾已下⻑期訂單 ， 4Q26營收攀升 ：
3Q26營收季增27.9%至15.6億元 ， 係因矽光產品出貨增加 ， 毛利率因營收規模
擴大上升1.11ppts至58.6%，營業費用較2Q26增加約1,400萬元至1.43億元，
主要來自研發費用以及隨獲利增加按比例提列之員工分紅費用，稅後EPS為
6.12元。4Q26矽光產品需求持續強勁，主要客戶皆已開始下6個⽉以上的⻑期
訂單，訂單能見度佳，隨公司本身與下游代工廠產能逐步開出，預期矽光產品
出貨量逐季成⻑，預估4Q26營收季增23.5%至19.3億元，稅後EPS為7.19元。  
設備與後段代工產能開出， 2027和2028年總產量連續兩年皆年增 150%：展
望2027~2028年，1.6T矽光產品已量產，3.2T與NPO/CPO應用之更高功率矽
光產品則和客戶持續合作開發和驗證中 ， 針對特定客戶之資料中心200G PD已
完成驗證且磊晶產能已規劃，預計未來數⽉將開始量產。目前E-Beam、奈米
壓印技術與DUV深紫外光微影三種技術皆同步進行擴產，隨更多E-Beam投
產，1Q27產能將明顯增加，奈米壓印設備預計於2026年底進廠。 
 投資風險 
設備與後段產能開出進度不如預期。

增27.9%至15.6億元 ， 係因矽光產品出貨量增加 ， 毛利率因營收規模擴大上升1.11ppts至58.6% ， 營業利益為7.71億元，
營業利益率為49.4%，營業費用較2Q26增加約1,400萬元至1.43億元，主要來自研發費用以及隨獲利增加按比例提列之
員工分紅費用，折舊費用較2Q26增加160萬元至7,660萬元，稅後淨利為6.21億元，稅後EPS為6.12元。4Q26矽光產品
需求持續強勁，主要客戶皆已開始下6個⽉以上的⻑期訂單，訂單能見度佳，隨公司本身與下游代工廠產能逐步開出，
預期矽光產品出貨量逐季成⻑，預估4Q26營收季增23.5%至19.3億元，稅後EPS為7.19元。影響毛利率因素有三：整體
營收與產出、匯率變化以及成本。管理層預期營收於接下來幾季將逐季成⻑，惟3Q26前半段台幣升值，隨後9⽉底台幣
轉貶值，匯率變化對3Q26毛利率產生些許不利影響，加上更多人員召募以及設備折舊成本逐步反映，且產品訂價方面，
聯亞僅維持賺取合理利潤，將無法持續大幅漲價，導致毛利率於接下來幾季成⻑幅度將趨緩。 
設備與後段代工產能開出，2027 和 2028 年總產量連續兩年皆年增 150%：展望 2027~2028 年，聯亞 1.6T 矽光產品
已經開始量產，3.2T 與 NPO/CPO 應用之更高功率矽光產品則和客戶持續合作開發和驗證中，另外過去受限產能，公
司設備主要用於生產 LD，自 2027 年開始，針對特定客戶之資料中心 200G 以上規格之 PD 已完成驗證，並已規劃相對
應磊晶產能，將於未來數⽉將開始量產，惟出貨量仍遠小於 LD。2026年 8 ⽉董事會決議與 1 美系資料中心客戶簽訂為
期 4 年之⻑期供貨合約，為滿足該客戶之需求，公司追加約 30 億元資本支出，該資本支出購置之設備可帶動 2027 和
2028 年總產量連續兩年皆年增 150%，另外因應磷化銦基板缺料問題，聯亞目前與 2 家日本主要供應商與 1 家美國主
要供應商達成未來數年的⻑期供貨合約，且美系客戶亦提供部分資金，協助確保後續大規模量產之基板供應無虞。因應
產能不足，聯亞持續新增後段外包合作夥伴位，台灣地區增加數家大型集團作為合作夥伴，該集團在光通訊領域投入意
願高，其資本支出承諾與實力遠高於早期合作夥伴，有助於與聯亞共同爭取國際 AI 訂單。曝光顯影製程方面，目前 E-
Beam、奈米壓印技術與 DUV 深紫外光微影三種技術皆同步進行擴產，首部新購置之 E-Beam 設備已於 2026 年 7 ⽉
開始量產，帶動 7 ⽉營收大幅躍升，隨更多 E-Beam 與 MOCVD 設備投產，1Q27 產能將有明顯增加且未來 8 個季度
產能將逐步增加 ， 奈米壓印設備預計於 2026 年底進場 ， DUV 深紫外光微影設備則於 2027 年以後加入生產 。 綜上所述 ，
預估 2026/2027/2028 年營收年增 155%/130%/86%至56.2/129.1/239.5 億元，稅後 EPS 為 21.01/49.3/97.15 元。

Document 253: _暂时安全、随时崩溃_！德银最新警告：全球市场正处于五大“危险错位”

Source PDF: all report pdf/_暂时安全、随时崩溃_！德银最新警告：全球市场正处于五大“危险错位”.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

德银最新报告揭⽰，全球市场正站在⼑刃上 —— 油价飙⾄ 113 美元、债市利差创年内新⾼，
股市却距历史峰值仅 3% 之遥。通胀升温⽽加息预期反降，债市承压⽽股市岿然不动，五⼤
跨资产定价错位正在悄然积累。德银警告，⻛险暂时 " 彼此抵消 " 的脆弱平衡⼀旦打破，跨资
产重估或将骤然来袭。
摘要：
全球市场正停留在⼀个极窄的着陆区：⻛险表⾯上仍被隔离，但油价、通胀、利率、债市与股
市之间的定价错位正在扩⼤，任何增⻓路径偏离都可能触发跨资产重估。
据追⻛交易台，在 11 ⽉ 2 ⽇发布的报告中，德银指出， 10 ⽉压⼒信号已经增多。上周五 S&P 500
指数收于六周低点，美国和欧洲债券利差在 10 ⽉⾛阔，美国两年期国债收益率录得逾⼀年最⼤
⽉度降幅，收益率⾛势已接近避险模式。
德银宏观策略师 Henry Allen 认为，当前组合需要⾮常窄的落点：股市仍相对坚挺，债市压⼒更
清晰，油价⾼企，市场只计⼊有限货币紧缩，⻓期通胀预期却保持受控。增⻓⾛弱会让股市难
以继续免受债市压⼒冲击，增⻓更具韧性则会推⾼收益率，并增加更快加息⻛险。

对投资者⽽⾔，关键已转向这些⻛险能否继续分隔。德银列出五⼤市场错位，核⼼问题在于，
市场已经为部分⻛险定价，却尚未为⻛险彼此传导留出⾜够缓冲。
油价推⾼短端通胀，利率路径转向鸽派
10 ⽉，随着市场担忧美伊冲突升级，油价继续上⾏。德银称， 2027 年 1 ⽉布伦特原油当前活跃合
约上⽉上涨 19% ，上周五收盘报 113 美元 / 桶。通胀定价同步升温，欧元区⼀年期通胀互换 10 ⽉
上升 27 个基点⾄ 3.86% ，美国⼀年期互换上升 14 个基点⾄ 2.63% 。
与此同步，央⾏利率定价却朝鸽派⽅向移动。市场计⼊的美联储 2027 年 6 ⽉会议利率上⽉下降 30
个基点，欧洲央⾏同期利率下降 22 个基点。德银认为，⾦融压⼒加重与增⻓放缓担忧可以解释
部分重定价，但在通胀⾼于⽬标时，央⾏转向宽松受到约束。2022 ⾄ 2023 年加息周期显⽰，即
便硅⾕银⾏倒闭及地区银⾏动荡带来压⼒，央⾏仍继续加息。
德银由此判断，投资者可能低估央⾏鹰派⻛险，因为通胀压⼒仍在累积，市场却减少了加息押

注。
油价回落仍被押注，⻓期通胀⼏乎未动
德银表⽰，今年油市⼀项持续特征是，期货曲线不断计⼊油价正常化，但这⼀正常化迟迟未出
现。美伊冲突初期，市场预期冲突会很快结束，这⼀逻辑可以成⽴。冲突延续后，布伦特期货
曲线的现货溢价结构仍然存在，前端合约与 12 个⽉合约之间的价差继续指向未来⼀年油价⼤幅
下滑。
⻓期通胀预期同样平静。欧元区五年期通胀互换上周五收于 2.46% ，仅略⾼于⽉初 2.43% ，仍低
于 4 ⽉峰值 2.54% 。德银称，市场基本未计⼊当前通胀冲击的⼆轮效应。
这种定价可以源⾃央⾏收紧政策压低通胀的预期，但过去⼀个⽉市场实际计⼊更宽松政策；也
可以源⾃⾼油价破坏需求并压低未来价格的判断，但较⻓期油价期货也已上涨，削弱这⼀解
释。德银提⽰，能源冲击持续越久，企业向其他部⻔传导成本的概率越⾼。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

债市承压，股市仍贴近纪录⾼位
近期压⼒率先在债市显现。上周五，美国⾼收益债利差升⾄ 340 个基点，为 2025 年 5 ⽉以来最
宽。美国投资级债利差收于 90 个基点，为 3 ⽉美伊冲突开始后最初⼀轮市场压⼒以来最⾼。欧洲
投资级与⾼收益债利差也接近 2025 年 5 ⽉以来最宽⽔平。
股市给出的信号温和得多。S&P 500 距离纪录⾼位仅约 3% ， VIX 逾三个⽉未收于 20 上⽅，
STOXX 600 距离⾃⾝纪录⾼位约 2% 。德银对⽐称，今年 3 ⽉美国投资级利差处于当前⽔平时，
S&P 500 较峰值跌逾 9% ； 2025 年 5 ⽉美国⾼收益利差处于当前⽔平时， S&P 500 较峰值跌近
8% 。
德银提醒，债券利差⾛阔会提⾼资本成本并收紧⾦融条件，削弱投资意愿，最终压制增⻓并伤
及股市。当前债市韧性意味着债市压⼒尚未充分传导。
欧洲股市跑赢，欧洲资产内部信号分裂
10 ⽉， STOXX 600 按本币计上涨 1.9% ，相较 S&P 500 和 Nikkei 的下跌表现更强，换算为美元后

也保持优势。
同⼀时期，欧洲其他资产释放更负⾯信号。欧洲⾼收益债利差 10 ⽉⾛阔 65 个基点，为 2022 年以
来最⼤单⽉扩张。欧元成为当⽉表现最弱的 G10 货币，对美元下跌 1.5% 。
欧洲科技板块权重较⼩可解释部分相对表现，因为全球科技股 10 ⽉承压。与此同时，欧洲⾃⾝
压⼒也很明显，法德 10 年期国债利差接近 2010 年代初欧债危机以来最宽⽔平。欧洲股市强势与
信⽤、主权利差和汇率⾛弱形成鲜明反差。
悲观叙事相互抵消，尾部⻛险暂被隔离
德银将过去⼀个⽉最突出的三类⻛险概括为：地缘政治担忧推⾼油价，围绕法国的财政政策担
忧推宽欧洲主权债利差， AI 增⻓担忧引发科技股抛售。
这些⻛险常被放在同⼀次避险交易中理解，但德银认为，它们之间存在⾃然对冲。若 AI 相关增⻓
担忧兑现，资本开⽀预期、⽣产率增⻓和股市⾛弱会压低政府债收益率，缓解近期财政压⼒。
较弱增⻓还会降低油品以及 AI 相关产品，诸如存储芯⽚的需求，减轻通胀与债市压⼒，并降低加
息概率。
同时，更紧的⾦融条件会承担⼀部分加息功能，帮助压低通胀，反过来减少央⾏继续加息的必

要性。德银称，⻛险相关性并未朝同⼀⽅向运⾏，这使最悲观结果同时发⽣的难度上升。

### Page 3

德银认为，当前均衡要求极窄着陆区。过去数周，⾦融压⼒仍保持分隔，部分原因在于主要⻛
险彼此抵消。这种抵消有上限。如果增⻓显著放缓，即使收益率和油价下⾏，股市也会失去⽀
撑。如果增⻓维持韧性，能源冲击会继续强化央⾏鹰派压⼒。
因此，最⼤错位可能正是⻛险仍被分隔本⾝：投资者已经接受更⾼油价，却未显著上调⻓期通
胀预期；市场已经接受更宽信⽤利差，却未给出近期压⼒阶段常⻅的股市跌幅。接下来数⽉，
全球资产能否维持这种隔离状态，将决定这轮 “ 暂时安全 ” 能否延续。

~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
以上精彩内容来⾃追⻛交易台。
更详细的解读，包括实时解读、⼀线研究等内容，请加⼊【追⻛交易台 ▪ 年度会员】

### Page 5

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

Document 272: Anthropic还在疯狂抢算力：8954亿美元承诺，连旧煤电厂都不放过

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic还在疯狂抢算力：8954亿美元承诺，连旧煤电厂都不放过.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

为了满⾜ Claude 模型训练和企业客⼾调⽤需求， Anthropic 不仅与亚⻢逊、⾕歌、微软等云
服务商签订⻓期协议，还开始直接采购芯⽚、参与数据中⼼建设，并向前加密货币矿企和
新型云服务商寻找电⼒与机房资源。
摘要：
AI 公司正在变成⼤型基础设施买家。
Anthropic 的扩张路径，已经从训练模型、争夺企业客⼾，延伸到建设数据中⼼、采购芯⽚和协
调电⼒资源。为了获得⾜够的计算能⼒，这家公司开始直接介⼊过去主要由云服务商和基础设
施运营商承担的⼯作。
美国当地时间 11 ⽉ 2 ⽇， Anthropic 向美国证券交易委员会提交更新后的招股书。⽂件补充了
2026 年第三季度相关财务数据，并披露⻓期采购和租赁安排。
⽂件显⽰，算⼒成为 Anthropic 扩张过程中最重要的成本项。为了满⾜ Claude 模型训练和企业客
⼾调⽤需求，该公司不仅与亚⻢逊、⾕歌、微软等云服务商签订⻓期协议，还开始直接采购芯
⽚、参与数据中⼼建设，并向前加密货币矿企和新型云服务商寻找电⼒与机房资源。

收⼊增⻓ 12 倍，重新出现经营亏损
更新后的招股书显⽰， Anthropic 第三季度营收为 169.6 亿美元，⾼于第⼆季度的 115.6 亿美元，
也远⾼于 2025 年同期的约 13.8 亿美元。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

今年前三个季度， Anthropic 累计营收 332.42 亿美元，去年同期为 25.45 亿美元，增⻓约 12 倍。
不过，营收增⻓并没有带来经营利润的同步改善。
第三季度， Anthropic 经营费⽤升⾄ 175 亿美元，第⼆季度为 106 亿美元。当季公司录得 5.67 亿美
元经营亏损，⽽第⼆季度曾实现 9.74 亿美元经营利润。计算及基础设施费⽤达到约 99 亿美元，
较第⼆季度的约 56 亿美元增加近⼀倍。
Anthropic 将经营利润转负主要归因于短期算⼒采购。由于客⼾需求增⻓超过此前锁定的算⼒供
应， Anthropic 不得不在市场上临时购买额外计算资源，⽽短期采购价格明显⾼于多年期协议。
第三季度， Anthropic 短期算⼒采购⾦额约 50 亿美元。此外，⼀项与员⼯捐赠配捐计划有关的⾮
现⾦费⽤约 20 亿美元，也对当季经营结果造成了影响。
剔除股权薪酬和上述捐赠配捐费⽤后， Anthropic 第三季度调整后经营利润率约为 15% ，低于第
⼆季度的 19% 。调整后利润率下降，反映出收⼊扩张过程中，算⼒采购和其他经营成本仍在侵

蚀利润空间。
价格端也出现变化。 Anthropic 披露，第⼆季度⾄第三季度，客⼾使⽤量增⻓带来的收⼊增加，
部分被新模型版本降价和合同折扣抵消，影响⾦额约 6 亿美元。
Anthropic 第三季度净亏损为 232.9 亿美元，低于第⼆季度的 663 亿美元。前三个季度累计净亏损
达到 1040.39 亿美元，但其中约 943 亿美元与可转换票据、衍⽣负债等⾦融⼯具公允价值变动有
关，主要受到公司估值上升影响。

### Page 3

8954 亿美元承诺，近⼀半将在 2030 年前兑现
招股书披露，截⾄ 9 ⽉底， Anthropic 通过采购承诺和租赁协议形成的累计⽀出承诺达到 8954 亿
美元，绝⼤部分与计算资源和基础设施有关。
其中，约 4475 亿美元预计在 2030 年 12 ⽉ 31 ⽇前兑现，其余部分延伸⾄ 2031 年⾄ 2045 年。未来
12 个⽉内到期的承诺约 698 亿美元。
这笔⾦额并不等于已经形成的有息债务，也不代表 Anthropic 需要⽴即⽀付全部款项。它包含不
同类型的采购合同、设备租赁和数据中⼼相关协议，付款时间分布在未来多年。但部分合同具
有较强约束⼒，即使实际使⽤量低于预期，公司仍可能需要承担相应付款义务。
从合同构成看，尚未开始的租赁承诺约 3214 亿美元，固定最低采购承诺约 2656 亿美元。这些⻓
期安排将未来的算⼒采购和基础设施使⽤成本提前锁定，也让公司承担了较⻓时间的付款责
任。
截⾄ 9 ⽉底， Anthropic 持有现⾦、现⾦等价物和短期投资合计 1318.45 亿美元。如果计⼊可⽤信

贷额度，公司总流动性约 1466 亿美元。

### Page 4

与账⾯资⾦相⽐，⻓期承诺的规模明显更⼤。 8954 亿美元约为 Anthropic 现⾦及短期投资的 6.8
倍；未来 12 个⽉约 698 亿美元的到期承诺，相当于现有现⾦及短期投资的约 53% 。
招股书也明确提⽰了其中的经营⻛险。 Anthropic 收⼊主要来⾃按使⽤量计费的消费型业务，⼤
部分收⼊没有⻓期合同保障，算⼒和基础设施⽀出却通常需要提前多年锁定。公司可能按照预
期收⼊签署⻓期协议，但未来实际收⼊未必达到预期。
如果客⼾收⼊增⻓⽆法覆盖相关付款，或者公司⽆法以可接受的条件继续融资， Anthropic 可能
⽆法履⾏部分合同。某些商业协议违约还可能触发信贷协议和可转换票据中的交叉违约条款，
导致债务提前到期，甚⾄使供应商终⽌合作。
成本增⻓已经体现在财务数据中。前三个季度， Anthropic 计算及基础设施费⽤同⽐增⻓ 329% ，
增加约 144 亿美元。第三季度相关费⽤约 99 亿美元，占当季营收约 58% 。
从云服务商到矿企， Anthropic 全球寻找算⼒

据外媒报道，去年年底， Anthropic 管理层已经意识到算⼒需求预测存在较⼤不确定性，随后密
集签署了数⼗份算⼒和基础设施协议，合作对象从⼤型科技公司延伸到数据中⼼初创企业、芯
⽚⼚商，以及曾经从事加密货币挖矿的企业。
这轮采购不再局限于购买云服务。 Anthropic 开始招聘数据中⼼建设和供应链专家，直接购买服
务器设备和冷却系统，帮助合作⽅加快机房建设。它还与多个州的数据中⼼开发商接触，研究
电⽹接⼊、项⽬许可，以及将旧煤电⼚和⼯业设施改造为数据中⼼的可能性。
新型云服务商和前加密货币矿企由此进⼊ Anthropic 的供应体系。外媒整合公开资料显⽰，
Fluidstack 、 Lambda 等公司提供算⼒资源， TeraWulf 、 Hut 8 等企业则参与数据中⼼建设。
这些合作⽅掌握着⼟地、电⼒接⼊资源或现成的数据中⼼设施。对 Anthropic ⽽⾔，利⽤已有场
地和电⼒资源，可以减少从零建设⼤型园区所需的时间。对部分前矿企来说，转向 AI 数据中⼼也
提供了新的业务增⻓空间。

但现成场地并不等于可以⽴即交付算⼒。电⼒供应、设备安装和施⼯⼈员都可能拖慢项⽬进
度。
Anthropic 最初寻找的数据中⼼项⽬规模⾄少在 200MW 左右，后来开始接受规模约 50MW 的项
⽬，再通过电池储能和天然⽓发电等⽅式补充供电。公司也考虑把多个⼩型项⽬组合起来，逐
步增加算⼒容量。
Anthropic 也开始直接参与基础设施项⽬的设计和采购。公司购买芯⽚、冷却系统等设备，再交
由合作运营商安装，以缩短从项⽬建设到算⼒上线的时间。
这套模式扩⼤了 Anthropic 可以接触的资源范围，但也增加了项⽬管理的复杂度。不同数据中⼼
的建设周期、设备规格、电⼒条件和交付时间并不⼀致。即使合同已经签署，实际算⼒仍要经
过建设、设备安装、并⽹和调试才能投⼊使⽤。

### Page 5

能源投资机构 Energize Capital 管理合伙⼈约翰 · 塔夫（ John Tough ）认为， Anthropic 并不掌握
完整的算⼒供应管线，只能在全球范围内逐个寻找电⼒和基础设施资源，再把不同规模的项⽬
拼接起来。
Anthropic 在招股书中将这部分专⽤基础设施称为 Anthropic Direct 。公司希望通过⻓期锁定专⽤
容量，减少对价格更⾼的短期算⼒采购的依赖。
外媒援引知情⼈⼠的话透露， Anthropic 预计 2026 年可拥有超过 3.5GW 的算⼒容量，并计划在
2027 年将规模扩⼤⼀倍。
Anthropic 的⻓期算⼒合同，集中在少数⼤型科技公司和基础设施供应商⼿中。
根据招股书及相关披露， Anthropic 与⾕歌的合同⾦额约 1213 亿美元，与亚⻢逊约 1100 亿美元，
与微软约 449 亿美元；与 SpaceX 的相关协议⾦额最⾼约 1205 亿美元，期限延伸⾄ 2036 年。此
外，该公司还与 Fluidstack 和 Lambda 签有相关协议。

博通相关设备租赁承诺约 1612 亿美元，也是此次算⼒⽀出结构中不能忽略的⼀部分。
此外， Anthropic 还计划额外采购约 1250 亿⾄ 1500 亿美元的⾕歌芯⽚，并预计在 AMD 芯⽚上的
⽀出超过 200 亿美元。这些安排没有固定的合同⾦额，因此不计⼊前述 8954 亿美元承诺总额。
多芯⽚架构是 Anthropic 降低供应⻛险的另⼀项安排。该公司在招股书中称，其算⼒资源来⾃
AWS Trainium 、⾕歌 TPU 、英伟达 GPU 和 AMD GPU ，并将不同来源的算⼒统⼀调度，根据不
同 任 务 对 价 格 和 性 能 的 要 求 分 配资 源 。 这 有 助 于 降 低 对 单 ⼀ 芯 ⽚ 供 应 商 的 依赖 ， 但 也 让
Anthropic 与供应链伙伴之间的关系更加复杂。

### Page 6

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

写评论
图⽚
亚⻢逊和⾕歌既是 Anthropic 的重要投资者，也是云服务和芯⽚资源供应商。微软同样提供云基
础设施。博通则参与芯⽚及设备供应。 Anthropic 向这些企业⽀付算⼒费⽤，同时⼜从部分企业
获得投资或融资⽀持。
Evercore ISI 分析师⻢克 · ⻢哈尼（ Mark Mahaney ）此前指出， Anthropic 与⼤型云服务商之间的
关系具有多重属性：既是客⼾，也是竞争者、销售渠道和投资对象。

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -29.18 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Dầu khí, Bảo hiểm và Dịch vụ phân phối là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -3,013 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã MCH, VIC và FPT; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở mức 
887 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -2,021 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -4,384 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng -
4,275 hợp đồng và đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -7,815 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam tiếp tục điều chỉnh tuần thứ tư liên tiếp, khi VN-Index lùi về đóng cửa 1.706 
điểm - giảm khoảng 1,7% so với tuần trước và đang dao động quanh vùng hỗ trợ tâm lý 
1.700 điểm, cũng là vùng đáy cũ của tháng 8. Thanh khoản thị trường tiếp tục suy yếu, 
phản ánh tâm lý thận trọng cao độ trong bối cảnh thị trường thiếu vắng thông tin hỗ trợ 
trong nước, trong khi các yếu tố bên ngoài như xung đột tại Iran, và chi phí vốn toàn cầu 
chưa hạ nhiệt tiếp tục đè nặng tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Khối ngoại duy trì chuỗi bán ròng kéo 
dài nhiều phiên liên tiếp, nối dài đà rút vốn đã lên tới xấp xỉ 95 nghìn tỷ đồng từ đầu năm.

tác giúp thị trường giao dịch cân bằng trở lại.

Source: Trading View, KB Securities Vietnam

Ngắn hạn 
(Dưới 3 tháng)

Trung hạn (Từ 3 đến 9 tháng)

Chỉ báo  Nhận định xu hướng Hành động 
Xanh đậm  Khả năng tăng điểm cao Mua mạnh 
Xanh nhạt  Khả năng tăng điểm vừa phải Mua từng phần 
Vàng  Xu hướng trung tính Mua/Bán trading 
Cam  Khả năng giảm điểm vừa phải Bán từng phần 
Đỏ  Khả năng giảm điểm cao Bán mạnh

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tiếp tục hình thành mẫu nến giảm điểm tiêu cực trên đồ thị tuần, đánh dấu tuần 
giảm thứ 4 liên tiếp, cho thấy áp lực phân phối vẫn đang có phần lấn át. Trên khung ngày, 
tuy chỉ số đã có nỗ lực phục hồi trở lại trong 3 phiên đan xen, đặc biệt là phản ứng trong 
phiên cuối tuần khi chạm trendline trung hạn, nhưng việc chỉ có thể hình thành các mẫu 
nến "spinning" hàm ý đà tích lũy chưa thực sự thuyết phục. Trong một vài phiên kế tiếp, 
VNINDEX có thể xuất hiện nhịp hồi kỹ thuật từ vùng hỗ trợ, tương ứng với cạnh dưới tam 
giác, nhưng sau khi chớm phá vùng hỗ trợ quanh 1710 và hiện chỉ còn một số ít các mã giữ 
được xu hướng tăng tính từ đáy tháng 7, xu hướng ngắn hạn đang trở nên khá trung tính.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1780 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1740 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1680 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1630 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Document 375: NZ labour market preview, Q3 2026 (2026-11-04)

### Page 1

Westpac New Zealand Economics
Turning point?
• We expect the unemployment rate to edge
down to 5.5% for the September quarter.
• Job numbers have been gaining momentum
in recent months, and now look to be enough
to outstrip population growth. However, the
participation rate will be the deciding factor.
• Despite cost-of-living pressures on workers,
the existing slack in the labour market is likely
to keep wage growth subdued.
• Our forecasts are a little more positive than

what the RBNZ assumed in their September
policy statement. Assuming they deliver on
an OCR hike this afternoon, the labour market
surveys could have an important bearing on
whether they follow up with another hike in
December.

The New Zealand economy has returned to growth over
the last year, despite a number of headwinds. That’s been
accompanied by some modest growth in jobs, though
to date it hasn’t been enough to prevent a further rise

in the unemployment rate. However, we think that next
Wednesday’s labour market reports could show the jobs
market reaching a turning point.
We expect the unemployment rate to ease from 5.6%
to 5.5% in the September quarter, which would mark
the first fall in almost five years. That’s not a firmly held
view, given the usual margin of uncertainty around this
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
Preview of Q3 labour market surveys:
4 November, 10:45am.
28 Oct 2026 | Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
measure. But we’re now seeing consistent evidence
that jobs growth is outstripping the modest pace of
population growth. We also note that the previous result
was 5.56% in unrounded terms, so it wouldn’t take much
to tip the rounded rate down to 5.5% this time.
On the whole, our forecasts are a little stronger than
what the RBNZ assumed in its September Monetary
Policy Statement. While our unemployment rate forecast

is identical, we’re more positive on both employment
and labour force participation. Meanwhile, our pick of a
0.5% rise in the Labour Cost Index is slightly ahead of the
RBNZ’s estimate of 0.4%.
Assuming that the RBNZ delivers the OCR hike that we
expect this afternoon, we think they will be noncommittal
about the chances of another hike at the 9 December
Monetary Policy Statement. In that respect, the labour
market surveys could be important in setting market

expectations for the December review, as there aren’t
that many major data releases between now and then.
Forecast details.
We expect the Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS)
to show a 0.5% rise in employment for the September
quarter. That’s broadly in line with the Monthly
Employment Indicator (MEI), with the September release
this morning showing a third straight month of growth
in filled jobs. We note that employment growth in the
HLFS has actually outstripped the MEI for the last nine

quarters, but it’s not clear why this has been the case and
we’re wary of extrapolating it too far into the next quarter.

Employment growth of around 0.5% would be more than
enough to absorb the modest 0.3% rise in the working-
age population, and on its own would suggest a fall in

the unemployment rate. But it also comes down to the
rate of labour force participation, which we don’t really
have much guidance on. The participation rate has been
choppy over recent quarters, and in the June quarter
we saw an unusually large 0.3pt rise to 70.7%; we’re
assuming some payback this time with a more modest
0.1pt rise to 70.8%.
We’ll also be keeping an eye on the broader indicators of
slack in the labour market, although we don’t formally use

these in our forecasts. The June quarter saw a sharp rise
in the underutilisation rate from 12.9% to 13.8%. However,
we think there’s some residual seasonality in this series,
relating to one component in particular: people who are
available for work but not actively looking. In that case,
we could see a substantial pullback in the underutilisation
measure this time.
Previously we’ve characterised the labour market as a
“low-fire, low-hire” environment, with much of the rise in

unemployment reflecting the difficulty of breaking into
the workforce, rather than job losses. There are some
signs of a thaw emerging, with job vacancies picking up
from their lows over the last year and business surveys
pointing to a lift in hiring intentions. But we still expect
that it will be a long road to recovery – our current
forecasts see the unemployment rate at 5% by the end of
next year.
While cost-of-living pressures are again rearing their

head as a major concern for workers, the existing degree
of slack in the labour market will continue to make it
difficult for them to push for bigger pay increases. We
expect a 0.5% rise in the Labour Cost Index (LCI) for
the September quarter, which would nudge the annual
growth rate up slightly to 2.1%. That said, we don’t have a
lot to go on for this forecast. The MEI includes a measure
of median weekly earnings, which has shown no sign of
acceleration so far – but this is only a very loose indicator

of wage pressures, as it will also reflect any changes in
the composition of jobs and pay rates.

Document 406: The Good the Bad and the Ugly for Rates in 2027

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Good the Bad and the Ugly for Rates in 2027.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

The spaghetti-western title neatly captures the three big forces
shaping long-end rates: the ‘good’ of AI-driven productivity, the
‘bad’ of elevated ﬁscal deﬁcits, and the ‘ugly’ of renewed
inﬂationary pressure from ongoing wars. By the middle of 2027,
however, things should start to look a little less ugly as inﬂation
falls

Padhraic Garvey, CFA, Michiel Tukker and Benjamin Schroeder
The good, the bad and the ugly: AI, ﬁscal deﬁcits and inﬂation take centre
stage in the 2027 rates outlook
Key calls and themes and 2027
US rates and a triple whammy of obstacles to overcome
In the US, our baseline view centres on the good, the bad and
the ugly causing trouble in the coming months, and right
through the ﬁrst quarter of 2027 at least, to prompt a return of
the 10yr US Treasury yield to the 5.25%-plus area (vs 4.9%
currently). We’d cap the absolute upside at 5.5%, as waiting
later in 2027 is a 2%-plus handle for GDP growth and inﬂation,
alongside a stable but not worsening debt dynamic narrative.

### Page 2

The fall in inﬂation here is key, as that allows the Federal
Reserve to step away and for the market to re-discount cuts.
That allows long-dated yields to edge back down, with the
10yr targeting 4.75% for starters and likely settling in the 4.5-
4.75% zone, which is still above normal, but rightfully so. We
don't call for it, but a decision to materially cut long-end
Treasury funding would push in the same direction.
Euro rates have good reason to be nervous

For euro rates, we also expect a bumpy ride at the start of
2027, whereby markets anxiously weigh each inﬂation reading
for signs of second-round eﬀects. If all goes well, we should see
a signiﬁcant repricing of the European Central Bank path,
helping the 2yr EUR swap rate down by some 50bp. The 10yr
rate will be stickier, however, as a global rise in real rates adds
upward pressure to longer euro rates. If inﬂation does not ease
as hoped, then we could see a stretch higher in rates from here.

In such a scenario, the 10yr swap rate could push well above
4%.
European bond spreads in a duel with the "bad"
Fiscal strains and election risk are putting eurozone bonds to
the test. France is the key fault line, while Bunds’ safe-haven
appeal masks mounting supply pressures. We see a turbulent
ﬁrst half of 2027, and any relief hinges on a credible path to
French ﬁscal consolidation
A delayed landing for GBP rates
Sterling rates are well above levels we consider fair value, but

trauma from turning too dovish too quickly is still on investors'
minds. Only once the Bank of England can prove its ability to
bring inﬂation closer to target (by the end of 2027) do we
pencil in a signiﬁcant repricing lower for GBP rates. Relatively
high sensitivity to US rates means we don't expect the path
lower to be a smooth one. And with gilt markets increasingly
reliant on risk-sensitive investors, we think a material term risk
premium remains justiﬁed.

Document 448: What lessons can we learn to keep European industry competitive from the measures taken by Germany, as the cost of electricity continues to turn the screw_ (2026-10-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/What lessons can we learn to keep European industry competitive from the measures taken by Germany, as the cost of electricity continues to turn the screw_ (2026-10-22).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
WHAT LESSONS CAN WE LEARN TO KEEP EUROPEAN INDUSTRY COMPETITIVE FROM
THE MEASURES TAKEN BY GERMANY, AS THE COST OF ELECTRICITY CONTINUES TO
TURN THE SCREW?
Marianne Mueller and Pascal Devaux
issue 19
21 October 2026
The 2022 energy crisis clearly highlighted the issue of electricity prices in Europe — and, more broadly, the issue of energy prices. As
a result, European manufacturers’ competitiveness has been severely undermined when compared with the United States and China.

By reigniting tensions over electricity prices, the conflict that began in the Middle East in late February 2026 further underscores the
continent’s vulnerability to fossil fuel imports, despite the expansion of renewable energy. Faced with an environment of potential sharp
and sudden price rises, European governments do not have the same room for manoeuvre.
While the additional costs borne by European manufacturers are mainly linked to the share of gas in national electricity mixes, we will

first illustrate that, in Germany, grid operation and maintenance costs, as well as taxation, are also contributing significantly to these
costs.
We will then explore the solutions proposed by the German government under the new European measures regulating state aid to
electricity-intensive industries.
Finally, our estimates will show that, while these measures make Germany more competitive against some European countries, they
do not place it on an equal competitive footing with the United States or China. Nevertheless, Germany has made significant progress

in decarbonising and making its electricity mix more flexible from a European perspective. This should help to ensure that electricity costs
are reduced in the medium term.

### Page 2

2
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoInsight 19 / 21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
worldWHAT LESSONS CAN WE LEARN TO KEEP EUROPEAN INDUSTRY COMPETITIVE FROM THE MEA-SURES TAKEN BY GERMANY, AS THE COST OF ELECTRICITY CONTINUES TO TURN THE SCREW?
GAS: A KEY FACTOR IN THE ADDITIONAL ELECTRICITY COSTS
FACED BY EUROPEAN INDUSTRY
European manufacturers are still paying significantly higher elec -
tricity costs than their American and Chinese competitors. In 2019,

there was a 30% difference between the prices charged to European
electricity-intensive industries (corresponding to the lower end of the
industrial price range) and the average US industrial price. In 2025,
it stood at nearly 100% according to the IEA (2026) 1, after peaking at
an average of 148% between 2022 and 2023. There is currently a 50%
difference with their Chinese competitors2.
The disruption to Russian gas supplies since 2022 is behind the rising

gas prices, particularly as the EU has had to turn to liquefied natural
gas3 (LNG), which is more expensive than gas transported via pipe -
line and, above all, exposes Europe to global gas market conditions.
The growth of LNG has, in fact, intensified competition between the
major consumption regions, primarily Europe and Asia. European and
Asian prices now tend to move more in tandem (Chart 1). However, in
China, the prevalence of coal — a widely available local resource —

and a more regulated electricity price help to keep industrial prices
lower. At the same time, the United States enjoys plentiful domestic
gas, which is produced at a low cost, driven by the sharp rise in shale
oil and gas production over the past decade. By contrast, since 2022,
Europe has been more exposed to the cost of imported gas (90% of its
total consumption, according to Eurostat), while the United States and
China have enjoyed plentiful domestic resources.

Fluctuations and the level of gas prices are passed on to Eu -
ropean electricity prices via the ‘merit order’ marginal pricing
mechanism, with the wholesale price determined by the margi -
nal cost of the last (and most expensive) electricity source called
upon to meet demand.
As a result, gas plays a decisive role, with gas-fired power sta -
tions setting the price in around 40% of cases in Europe4, with si-
gnificant variations depending on national electricity mixes. It is

close to this average in Germany, below it in Spain (26%) and
France (17%), and above it in Italy (73%) 5 in 2025 6 7. Therefore,
electricity prices are still sensitive to fluctuations in gas prices,
even when electricity is not generated from gas (Chart 2). This
pass-through is particularly pronounced when demand rises
and requires the use of more expensive sources, or when supply
constraints arise (for example, during extreme heatwaves that
reduce wind-power generation or disrupt the cooling of nuclear

power plants).
1 Prices – Electricity 2026 – Analysis – IEA
2 The difference may be even larger for other manufacturers who do not receive the same support as electricity-intensive sectors.
3 Imports of Russian gas (via pipeline and LNG) accounted for around 45% of total European imports in 2021, compared with 14% in Q2 2026 (Bruegel, natural gas imports).
4 ACER, 2026, Key developments in EU electricity and gas markets, 2026 Monitoring report.
5 In Italy, in response to sharply rising gas prices in 2026, the government is currently negotiating a potential option of capping the prices of gas supplied to power stations in

order to limit the rise in the wholesale price of electricity. However, this can only be a temporary general mechanism, with the potentially major cost being borne by public funds
and/or households.
6 Eurelectric, 2026, Power Barometer.
7 EMBER, 2026, Latest energy shock reminds Europe of its risky reliance on gas.
8 Energy shock: 2026 vs. 2022 dashboard – 2nd October 2026 – Economic Studies – BNP Paribas
However, for the time being, the 2026 energy crisis is having a far

smaller effect on electricity prices than in 2022. On the one hand, the
2026 peak reached by the European TTF benchmark is still well below
that recorded in 20228.
In addition, there has been significant progress in decarbonising Eu -
ropean electricity mixes since 2022, which has helped to mitigate, on
average, the pass-through of gas prices to electricity prices.

SOME EUROPEAN COUNTRIES HAVE MANAGED TO STAY
COMPETITIVE, BUT GERMANY IS STILL THE EXCEPTION
Electricity prices partly vary due to countries’ electricity mixes
(Chart 3). Electricity-intensive manufacturers in countries where the
share of fossil energy is high face higher costs (Poland, Italy and Ire -
land) (Chart 4). Conversely, countries with a less carbon-intensive mix

(particularly France and Spain in recent times) are less directly ex -
posed to these fluctuations, with costs closer to the United States and
China (Chart 5).
The situation in Germany is paradoxical, as, despite progressively
greening its electricity mix and wholesale electricity prices (averaging
90 EUR/MWh over the 2023–2025 period) and average electricity costs
(across all industrial sectors) in line with the European average, the most
energy-intensive industries face higher costs than the European average

(and even more so compared with international competitors). This is
mainly due to grid costs and a tax burden above EU standards (Chart 6).
9 Eurostat data are used to compare prices with the rest of the EU. However, given that electricity prices are not uniform for businesses at a European level, there may be differences
between Eurostat and national sources. Jäger P ., 2025, “Cautionary tale or model for Europe? How member states should react to Germany’s new electricity subsidy”, Hertie School,

Jacques Delors Centre.
10 P . Devaux, 2026, “European Union: low carbon transition and energy sovereignty, a path fraught with obstacles”
In the first half of 2026, for the most electricity-intensive industries,
the difference compared with the European average was 9%, based on
the prices excluding taxes and levies provided by Eurostat. This gap is
mainly due to the cost of accessing the grid. If all taxes and levies are
included, the difference rises to 21% (Chart 7)9.

EUROPE’S APPROACH OF ADOPTING BOTH STRUCTURAL
PROGRAMMES AND TARGETED SUPPORT MEASURES
European programmes to support the low-carbon transition pursue
objectives relating to climate action (reducing greenhouse gas emis -
sions), sovereignty10 (increasing energy self-sufficiency) and competi -
tiveness (reducing the volatility and costs associated with fossil fuels).
The RePowerEU programme, which was launched in 2022 in response
to the energy crisis, primarily aims to reduce imports of Russian gas

and, more generally, to accelerate the low-carbon transition. Although
Europe has reduced its dependence on imports of Russian fossil fuels,
there has still not been enough progress around the energy transition
and cross-border connectivity. This is due to governance constraints

and struggles in implementing the programme in practice within each
Member State11.
State aid is still the main vehicle for this. In response to the 2021–
2022 energy crisis, the European Union temporarily relaxed the rules
on this type of aid in order to support businesses facing rising energy
prices. Any intervention of this kind, provided that it is selective and

could affect trade within the single market, must be authorised in ad -
vance by the European Commission in principle. Since June 2025, the
Clean Industrial Deal State Aid Framework (CISAF) has provided a more
sustainable framework for these kinds of interventions. In particular,
this framework, which runs until the end of 2030, authorises Member
States to grant temporary aid to electricity-intensive businesses to off-
set the cost of electricity. The aid must be targeted and come with

decarbonisation commitments12. Against the backdrop of the crisis in
the Middle East, the temporary METSAF scheme, adopted in April 2026,
helped (in relation to measures falling under the CISAF) to increase the
proportion of additional costs offset by governments from 50% to 70%,
thereby offering additional support to the most at-risk sectors.
GERMANY IS STILL SUFFERING FROM INSUFFICIENT IN-
VESTMENT IN ITS ELECTRICITY NETWORKS, WHICH THE
AID SCHEMES ARE ATTEMPTING TO OFFSET

The issue of electricity costs is a particularly hot issue in Germany,
given the importance of industry to the economy (around 20% of va -
lue added, one of the highest levels in Europe) and its exposure to
international competition. Furthermore, the delay in modernising
electricity infrastructure (only 22.6% of the high-voltage lines planned
for 2025 had actually been completed by that date) is causing bottle -
necks between the production areas in the north and the consump -

11 European Court of Auditors, 2026, “Implementation of the REPowerEU plan must be stepped up”.
12 It applies to the most electricity-intensive activities, which are seeing their competitiveness threatened by international competition, and follows the 4x50 principle: 50% of
electricity consumption is eligible for a subsidy; Of the eligible proportion, the subsidy may not exceed 50% of the wholesale price;
The final wholesale price paid by the company must not be less than €50/MWh;

50% of the aid received must be reinvested in decarbonisation (renewable energy generation, storage and improvements in energy efficiency); Around 90 sectors are covered by this
reform, accounting for approximately 2,000 companies, including in the chemical, steel and paper industries. The scheme, which is strictly regulated, will run for just three years
(from 1st January 2026 to the end of 2028) at an estimated total cost of between EUR 3 and 5 bn, financed by the special Climate and Transformation Fund.

13 Bundesnetzagentur Monitoring Report (2015) and Bundesnetzagentur Monitoring Report (2025). These costs mainly cover the costs of physical infrastructure; operational,
regulatory and compliance costs; and costs associated with using the transmission and distribution network.
14 The Industriestrompreis (industrial electricity price) is the public subsidy mechanism aiming to reduce the price of electricity for some industries.
15 The indirect carbon cost compensation mechanism is a government support measure for electricity-intensive industries, which aims to offset the cost of carbon-emission

allowances for energy producers.
tion centres in the south. As a result, the costs of accessing services
have increased fivefold over 10 years (rising from EUR 1 bn in 2014
to EUR 5.2 bn in 2024 13). At the same time, as we have already seen,
taxation and grid charges are hitting industry hard, with levies on large
consumers far exceeding the European average despite some support
mechanisms being put in place for the most electricity-intensive com-

panies (such as the Bandlastprivileg measure to reduce grid tariffs).
As a result, German industrial production has suffered in particular
due to rising energy prices in recent years, including electricity, with
electricity-intensive industries being especially affected (Chart 8).
In order to cope with these higher electricity costs than the European
average, Germany has been using European support schemes to reduce
the cost of electricity for businesses, particularly since the Merz go -

vernment took office in May 2025. The main scheme — the Industries-
trompreis14, made possible by the CISAF — has been approved by the
European Commission for the 2026–2028 period. While it is less bene-
ficial than the indirect carbon cost (ICC) offsetting included in electri -
city prices15, it has a broader scope, meaning that businesses ineligible
for the ICC can benefit from it. Other measures complement this targe-
ted support by addressing other components of the electricity bill. As a

result, the electricity tax has been set at the European minimum for
manufacturing sectors, while the gas-storage tax has been abolished.
The government is also subsidising grid fees, which should halve them.
Overall, according to our estimates based on 2024 prices, the final
price of electricity for these industries could fall by around a quarter
(to 116 EUR/MWh for industries with annual consumption of between
70 and 150 GWh, compared with a European average of 123 EUR/MWh)

(Chart 9). Germany’s advantage over Central and Eastern European
countries, where electricity costs are generally already higher, looks
set to increase.

However, even when taking all of the subsidies into account, the price
of electricity in Germany would remain higher than the levels seen in
France, Spain or Portugal (and even more so in the United States and
China).
FISCAL MEASURES: LIMITED ROOM FOR MANOEUVRE
There are a number of limitations to these European schemes. For
the country implementing them, lower electricity costs mean that

its industry becomes more competitive against industries from other
EU countries, but this does very little to address the disadvantage that
it faces compared with the United States and China. Therefore, this
measure will unlikely be enough to counteract the loss of market share
seen vis-à-vis the United States and China. In addition, the support
schemes are restrictive (conditional on investment in the low-carbon
transition) and are transitional. Finally, their cost is partly passed on

to the public purse or to consumers who do not receive this support
(in Germany, the cost of the new energy price support measures is
estimated at EUR 14.3 bn in 2026, or 0.3% of GDP16).
Some European manufacturers have proposed abolishing or reducing
the carbon tax as a solution to getting electricity prices down quickly.
In Germany, this tax accounted for 14% of the electricity price in the
first half of 2025, which is higher than the EU average (11%). However,

this proposal is already covered by the indirect carbon cost offsetting
mechanism for electricity-intensive industries exposed to carbon al -
lowance prices. In addition, while adjustments are being made — no -
tably to slow the rate at which allowances are reduced — it seems
highly unlikely that the scheme will be significantly challenged. It is
one of the cornerstones of Europe’s policy to combat climate change,
which has been effective thus far, as shown by the falling greenhouse

gas emissions from industry. More broadly, the gradual introduction of
a carbon border adjustment mechanism (CBAM) should mitigate com-
petition distortions caused by environmental practices17.
IN GERMANY AND THE REST OF EUROPE, HEAVY INVEST -
MENT IS DRIVING UP ELECTRICITY BILLS IN THE SHORT
TERM
In response to the challenge of the low-carbon transition and indus -
trial competitiveness, European governments must strike a balance
between decarbonising the electricity mix and ensuring a supply of

energy that is flexible enough (particularly from controllable sources)
to guarantee stable electricity systems. The integration of an increa -
sing proportion of intermittent renewable energy sources requires
massive investment in transmission and storage infrastructure (statio-
nary batteries). This expenditure, which places a long-term burden on
grid costs, reduces the scope for support schemes (particularly public
ones) to address competitiveness gaps. In Germany, the cost of the

transition is estimated at between EUR 122 bn, according to the go -
vernment (2.8% of GDP), and EUR 200 bn, according to the DIHK18 (4.6%
of GDP), by 2030–2035. If we take the ECB’s assumption19 that govern-
ments will cover around 17% of these investments, the annual public
16 The Industriestrompreis is estimated at around EUR 1.3 bn per year, the subsidy for network charges is projected to amount to EUR 6.5 bn in 2026, the abolition of the gas sur -

charge around EUR 3.4 bn, and the reduction in the electricity tax nearly EUR 3 bn per year. Furthermore, the ICC had cost EUR 2.3 bn in 2024 for costs incurred in 2023.

expenditure burden of the transition in Germany would be between
0.5 and 0.8 percentage points of GDP by 2030. However, this budgetary
pressure comes at the same time as three other budgetary increases:
rising social expenditure linked to an ageing population; the necessary
increase to defence budgets to meet NATO’s target of 3.5% of GDP by
2029; and the rising cost of debt-servicing20.
Germany has already made significant progress in maximising its use

of renewable energy, putting it at the head of the pack in Europe. The
country generates 20% of Europe’s renewable electricity (solar and
wind) and accounts for 30% of installed storage capacity (BESS) 21, far
ahead of other European countries (ranking third globally). However,
despite this momentum, Germany’s dependence on gas has not yet
decreased. As a matter of fact, it is only partially offsetting the planned
closure of coal-fired power stations and the phasing out of nuclear

reactors. Furthermore, the intermittent nature of solar and wind power
could prolong this dependence in the short and medium term. In order
to ensure that the German electricity system is flexible, new invest -
ments in gas-fired power plants, supported by public subsidies, are
planned22.

SUBSIDIES ARE HELPING TO ADDRESS THE EMERGENCY
CAUSED BY ENERGY CRISES WITHOUT RESTORING THE
INTERNATIONAL COMPETITIVENESS OF ENERGY-INTEN-
SIVE INDUSTRIES
In response to the impact of energy crises on industrial competitive -
ness, European governments are finding themselves having to strike a
delicate balance between providing short-term support to industries

exposed to increased international competition and continuing — if
not, accelerating — energy transition policies which will result in hi -
gher costs for electricity-network users for several years to come.
Beyond direct economic support measures, which are difficult to sus -
tain long term, four priority areas are emerging:
1/ Demonstrating flexibility in implementing European directives in
order to factor in national specificities and the differing timeframes

between energy crises (which can lead to irreversible industrial conse-
quences) and reforms (which have a longer-term horizon). The relaxa-
tion of the European carbon tax mechanism 23 appears to be a step
towards this.
2/ Continuing efforts to decarbonise the electricity mix, which are still
the main vehicle for sustainably reducing electricity prices for Euro -
pean industries in the medium and long term.
3/ Optimising the use of electricity networks on the demand side, in

particular through smart grids, a flagship element of the Commission’s
COM (2026) 600 reform presented last July24.
4/ Coordinating European electricity networks in order to harness the
complementarities between national systems.
Article completed on 19 October 2026
